Geometric Properties of Real Gromov-Witten Invariants
Penka Georgieva and Aleksey Zinger

Abstract
We collect geometric properties of the all-genus real Gromov-Witten theory and provide updates on its development since its introduction in 2015. We bring attention to a modification of the original construction of this theory which is applicable in a broader setting, obtain properties of the orientations of the moduli spaces of real maps under this modification which had previously been obtained for the original construction, and compare such orientations obtained via the two constructions when both constructions are applicable.’ In particular, the orientation of the moduli space of real maps from a disjoint union of domains may not be the product orientation of the moduli spaces of real maps from its components, a possibility overlooked in our past work.



1
Introduction

The foundations of
complex
Gromov-Witten (or GW-) theory, i.e. of counts
of
J
-holomorphic curves in symplectic manifolds, were established in the 1990s
and have been spectacularly applied in symplectic topology,
algebraic geometry, and string theory since then.
On the other hand, the progress in
real
GW-theory,
i.e. of counts of
J
-holomorphic curves in symplectic manifolds
preserved by anti-symplectic involutions, has been much slower,
as its mathematical foundations in positive genera did not even exist
until the mid 2010s.
Since the introduction of real positive-genera GW-theory in
[
17
]
,
its has been extended to broader settings in
[
10
]
by modifying
the approach of
[
17
]
to orienting the moduli spaces of stable real maps.
The present paper obtains geometric properties of the resulting orientations
analogous to the geometric properties obtained in
[
18
]
for
the orientations constructed in
[
17
]
and
compares the orientations obtained via the approaches of
[
17
]

and
[
10
]
when both are applicable.
We also update on developments in real GW-theory since its introduction
and correct some oversight in past work.
We hope that the present paper and its more algebraic companion
[
20
]

will facilitate further development of real GW-theory.

A
real manifold
is a pair
(X,\phi)
consisting of a (smooth) manifold
X

and an involution
\phi
on
X
, i.e.
\phi
is a diffeomorphism of
X
such that
\phi^{2}\!=\!\textnormal{id}_{X}
.
In such a case, we denote by
X^{\phi}\!\subset\!X
the fixed locus of
\phi
.
A
real bundle pair

(V,\varphi)
over a real manifold
(X,\phi)
consists
of a complex vector bundle
V\!\longrightarrow\!X
and a conjugation
\varphi
on
V
lifting
\phi
,
i.e.
\varphi^{2}\!=\!\textnormal{id}_{V}
and

\varphi\!:V_{x}\!\longrightarrow\!\overline{V}_{\phi(x)}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphism for every
x\!\in\!X
.
In such a case,
V^{\varphi}\!\longrightarrow\!X^{\phi}
is a real vector bundle.
For a real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)
over
(X,\phi)
, we denote by

\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}(V,\varphi)=\big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}V,\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\varphi\big)

the top exterior power of
V
over
\mathbb{C}
with the induced conjugation.
Direct sums, duals, and tensor products over
\mathbb{C}
of real bundle pairs over
(X,\phi)

are again real bundle pairs over
(X,\phi)
.

If
(X,\phi)
is a real manifold and
L\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a complex vector bundle, the homomorphisms

\displaystyle\phi_{L}^{\oplus}\!:L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},

\displaystyle\quad\phi_{L}^{\oplus}(v,w)

\displaystyle=(w,v),\qquad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle\phi_{L}^{\otimes}\!:L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},

\displaystyle\quad\phi_{L}^{\otimes}(v\!\otimes\!w)

\displaystyle=w\!\otimes\!v,

are conjugations covering
\phi
.
The homomorphism

\big(L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\phi_{L}^{\oplus}}\longrightarrow L|_{X^{\phi}},\quad(v,w)\longrightarrow v,

of real vector bundles over
X^{\phi}
is then an isomorphism.
We will call the orientation on the domain of this isomorphism induced
from the complex orientation of the target
the projection orientation

of
(L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L})^{\phi_{L}^{\oplus}}
.
If
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\!=\!1
, then

\big(L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},\phi_{L}^{\otimes}\big)\longrightarrow\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},\phi_{L}^{\oplus}\big),\qquad v\!\otimes\!w\longrightarrow-\mathfrak{i}(v,0)\!\wedge\!(0,w),

(1.1)

is an isomorphism of real bundle pairs over
(X,\phi)
.
The induced isomorphism

\begin{split}&\big\{rv\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!v\!\in\!L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\!:v\!\in\!L|_{X^{\phi}},\,r\!\in\!\mathbb{R}\big\}\!=\!\big(L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\phi_{L}^{\otimes}}\\
&\hskip 144.54pt\longrightarrow\big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\big)\!\big)^{\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\phi_{L}^{\oplus}}\!=\!\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(\!\big(L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\phi_{L}^{\oplus}}\big)\end{split}

of real line bundles over
X^{\phi}
intertwines the canonical orientation on the domain
(given by the standard orientation of
\mathbb{R}
) and the projection orientation on
the target.

Definition 1.1
.

Let
(X,\phi)
be a real manifold.
A
real orientation

(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
on a real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)
over
(X,\phi)

consists of

(RO1)

a complex line bundle
L\!\longrightarrow\!X
such that

w_{2}(V^{\varphi})=w_{2}(L)|_{X^{\phi}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}(V,\varphi)\approx\big(L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},\phi_{L}^{\otimes}\big),

(1.2)

(RO2)

a homotopy class
[\psi]
of isomorphisms
of real bundle pairs in (
1.2
), and

(RO3)

a spin structure
\mathfrak{s}
on the real vector bundle

V^{\varphi}\!\oplus\!L^{*}|_{X^{\phi}}
over
X^{\phi}

compatible with the orientation induced by
[\psi]
.

By
(RO2)
, a real orientation on
(V,\varphi)
determines a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}V^{\varphi}=\big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}V\big)^{\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\varphi}\approx\big(L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\phi_{L}^{\otimes}}=\big\{rv\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!v\!\in\!L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}\!:v\!\in\!L|_{X^{\phi}},\,r\!\in\!\mathbb{R}\big\}

(1.3)

and thus an orientation on
V^{\varphi}
.
In particular, the real vector bundle
V^{\varphi}\!\oplus\!L^{*}|_{X^{\phi}}
over
X^{\phi}

in
(RO3)
is oriented.
By the first condition in (
1.2
), it admits a spin structure.
By
[
14
, Theorems 1.1,1.2]
, a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})

on a rank
k
real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)
over a symmetric surface
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
possibly nodal and disconnected, determines a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\big(V\!\oplus\!(L^{*}\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*}),\varphi\!\oplus\!\sigma_{L^{*}}^{\oplus}\big)\approx\big(\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{k+2},\sigma\!\times\!\mathfrak{c}\big)

(1.4)

of real bundle pairs over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
where
\mathfrak{c}
is the standard conjugation on
\mathbb{C}^{k+2}
;
see Section
2
for the terminology.
This homotopy class of isomorphisms is one of the key ingredients
in the construction of real GW-invariants in
[
17
]
;
see also
[
15
]
and Section
3.3
.

A
real symplectic manifold
is a triple
(X,\omega,\phi)
consisting
of a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
and an anti-symplectic involution
\phi
on
X
,
i.e.
\phi^{*}\omega\!=\!-\omega
.
The fixed locus
X^{\phi}
of
\phi
is then a Lagrangian submanifold of
(X,\omega)
and

TX^{\phi}=\big\{\dot{x}\!\in\!TX|_{X^{\phi}}\!:\textnormal{d}\phi(\dot{x})\!=\!\dot{x}\big\}.

A
real orientation
on a real symplectic manifold
(X,\omega,\phi)

is a real orientation on the real bundle pair
(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)
over
(X,\phi)

in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
We call a real symplectic manifold admitting a real orientation
real-orientable
.
The anti-holomorphic involutions

\displaystyle\tau_{n}\!:\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{n-1},

\displaystyle\tau_{n}\big([[Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}]\big)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}[\overline{Z}_{2},\overline{Z}_{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}_{n},\overline{Z}_{n-1}],&\hbox{if}~n\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z};\\
[\overline{Z}_{2},\overline{Z}_{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}_{n-1},\overline{Z}_{n-2},\overline{Z}_{n}],&\hbox{if}~n\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z};\end{cases}

\displaystyle\eta_{2m}\!:\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{2m-1},

\displaystyle\eta_{2m}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{2m}]\big)

\displaystyle=\big[\overline{Z}_{2},-\overline{Z}_{1},\ldots,\overline{Z}_{2m},-\overline{Z}_{2m-1}\big],

are anti-symplectic with respect to the Fubini-Study symplectic
forms
\omega_{n}
and
\omega_{2m}
on the complex projective spaces
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

and
\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}
, respectively.
The real symplectic manifolds
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\omega_{n},\tau_{n})
with
n\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and

(\mathbb{P}^{2m-1},\omega_{2m},\eta_{2m})
admit real orientations
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})

with
L\!=\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}(n/2)
and
L\!=\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}}(m)
, respectively.
Many other examples of compact real symplectic manifolds with real orientations
are described in
[
19
, Section 1.1]
;
see also Section
4.1
in the present paper.
These include many projective complete intersections,
such as the real quintic threefolds,
i.e. the smooth hypersurfaces in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
cut out by degree 5 homogeneous polynomials
on
\mathbb{C}^{5}
with real coefficients;
they play a prominent role in the interactions of symplectic topology
with string theory and algebraic geometry.

For a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
, we denote by
\mathcal{J}_{\omega}

the contractible space of
\omega
-tamed almost complex structures on
X
and by

c_{1}(X,\omega)\!\in\!H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
the first Chern class of the complex vector bundle
(TX,J)

over
X
.
For a real symplectic manifold
(X,\omega,\phi)
, let

\mathcal{J}_{\omega}^{\phi}=\big\{J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}\!:\phi^{*}J\!=\!-J\big\}.

For
g\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
,
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,
B\!\in\!H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
, and
J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}^{\phi}
,
we denote by
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

the moduli space of stable real
J
-holomorphic degree
B
maps
from closed, possibly nodal and disconnected, symmetric Riemann surfaces of arithmetic genus
g

with
\ell
conjugate pairs of marked points.

Theorem 1.2
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
is a real-orientable
2n
-manifold with
n\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}

and
J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}^{\phi}
.

(1)

A real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
on
(X,\omega,\phi)
induces orientations
on the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

for all
g,\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
and
B\!\in\!H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
.

(2)

The induced orientations satisfy the properties stated in
Propositions
3.12
-
3.16
and
3.18
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
,
the (virtual) dimensions of all moduli spaces
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
,

\dim^{\textnormal{vir}}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\equiv(1\!-\!g)(n\!-\!3)\!+\!2\ell\!+\!\big\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\big\rangle,

(1.5)

are even. In particular, the product orientation on the Cartesian product of two such spaces
is independent of their ordering.

The notion of real orientation provided by
[
17
, Definition 5.1]

and used in
[
17
]
to orient moduli spaces of stable real maps
replaces
(L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},\phi_{L}^{\otimes})
and

(L^{*}\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\phi_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})
in (
1.2
) and (
1.4
)
by
(L,\widetilde{\phi})^{\otimes 2}
and
2(L,\widetilde{\phi})^{*}
, respectively,
for a real line bundle pair
(L,\widetilde{\phi})
over
(X,\phi)
.
The real vector bundle
(2L)^{2\widetilde{\phi}}\!=\!2L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
over
X^{\phi}

is then canonically oriented by taking the same orientation on both copies of
L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
.
The homomorphism

\Phi_{L}\!:2(L,\widetilde{\phi})\longrightarrow\big(L\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},\phi_{L}^{\oplus}\big),\quad\Phi_{L}(v,w)=\big(v\!+\!\mathfrak{i}w,\widetilde{\phi}(v\!-\!\mathfrak{i}w)\!\big),

(1.6)

is an isomorphism of real bundle pairs over
(X,\phi)
.
The restriction

\Phi_{L}\!:(2L)^{2\widetilde{\phi}}\longrightarrow\big(L\!\oplus\!\overline{L}\big)^{\phi_{L}^{\oplus}}

(1.7)

intertwines the canonical orientation on its domain
and the projection orientation on its target.
Definition
1.1
,
which is a slightly reworded version of the notion of
twisted (real) orientation data

introduced in
[
10
, Definition A.1]
,
thus weakens (broadens) the notion of real orientation of
[
17
, Definition 5.1]
.
In particular,
(\mathbb{P}^{2m-1},\omega_{2m},\eta_{2m})
with
m\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
admits
a real orientation in the sense of Definition
1.1
in the present paper,
but not in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 5.1]
.
If
(X,\phi)\!=\!(\Sigma,\sigma)
is a symmetric surface,
the compositions of the isomorphisms
\textnormal{id}\!\oplus\!\Phi_{L^{*}}

and (
1.4
) yields a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\big(V\!\oplus\!2L^{*},\varphi\!\oplus\!2\widetilde{\sigma}^{*}\big)\approx\big(\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{k+2},\sigma\!\times\!\mathfrak{c}\big)

(1.8)

of real bundle pairs over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
.

As noted in
[
10
]
, the construction of orientations on the moduli spaces of real maps
in
[
17
]
goes through almost verbatim with the weaker notion of real orientation
of Definition
1.1
; see also Section
3.3
.
However, the resulting orientations of the moduli spaces are generally different
when both constructions apply;
the orientations on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
arising from
the two constructions in such cases are compared via Lemma
3.1
with

\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!=1\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\deg L=-\big\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\big\rangle/2.

Propositions
3.12
,
3.13
, and
3.18

compare the orientations of various moduli spaces under the orientating procedure
described in Sections
3.3
.
Propositions
3.14
and
3.16
do the same for
the codimension 2 strata of maps with a conjugate pair of nodes in the domain
and the codimension 1 strata of maps with an isolated real node, respectively.

Propositions
3.13
,
3.14
, and
3.18

are analogues of Theorems 1.4, 1.2, and 1.5, respectively, in
[
18
]
,
which apply to the orientating procedure used in
[
17
]

(the statements of these theorems are also incorporated into the statements of
Propositions
3.13
,
3.14
, and
3.18
).
On the other hand, Proposition
3.12
concerns the behavior of
the orientations of the moduli spaces of real maps under
the disjoint union of the domains of the maps.
Contrary to the expectation, the orientation at the disjoint union of two maps
may not be the product of the orientations at the two maps.
This possibility was overlooked in our past work, resulting in oversights in some proofs,
but fortunately these oversights do not impact any material statements;
see Section
4
.
Proposition
3.16
likewise has no analogue in our past work;
it had not been needed for the previously completed work in real positive-genus GW-theory,
but is used in
[
20
]
.

Similarly to the GW-invariants of a compact symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
,
the real GW-invariants of a compact real symplectic manifold
(X,\omega,\phi)

with a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
are rational numbers,
but an invertible transformation determined by the topology of
(X,\phi)
is
expected to convert these numbers into integers.
If the (real) dimension of
X
is 6, such a transformation is expected to be linear.
This is confirmed in
[
25
]
for “Fano” classes
in a compact real symplectic sixfold with a real orientation,
adapting the result of
[
28
, Theorem 1.5]
from the complex GW-theory
to the real GW-theory; see also Section
4.4
.
The structure of such a transformation for the complex GW-invariants of
a compact Calabi-Yau symplectic sixfold is predicted
in
[
21
]
based on string theory considerations,
mathematically motivated in
[
3
,
4
]
, and confirmed in
[
22
]
.
The analogue of this transformation for the real GW-invariants of
a compact Calabi-Yau symplectic sixfold with an anti-symplectic involution
is predicted in
[
2
,
27
]
based on string theory considerations
and mathematically motivated in
[
10
]
;
its mathematical confirmation is the subject of
[
12
]
.
Such a transformation for the complex genus 1 GW-invariants of
a compact Calabi-Yau symplectic tenfold is mathematically motivated in
[
26
]
,
but its adaptation to the real setting is yet to be obtained.
Other important geometric problems in real GW-theory include the mirror symmetry predictions
of
[
27
]
.
More algebraic directions for future research are indicated in
[
20
]
.

We gather the most frequently used notation and terminology in Section
2
.
In Section
3.1
, we describe the behavior of
natural orientations on the determinants of real Cauchy-Riemann operators
on real bundle pairs of the form
(L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})

over symmetric Riemann surfaces
(\Sigma,\sigma)
under various operations.
We use this to study the behavior of the orientations
on the determinants of real Cauchy-Riemann operators induced by real orientations
in Section
3.2

and the orientations on the moduli space of real maps in
Sections
3.3
and
3.4
,
establishing Propositions
3.12
-
3.16
in the process.
In Section
3.5
,
we specialize to genus 0 and establish Proposition
3.18
.
In Section
4
, we extend other results from our past work to the broader setting
of Definition
1.1
and correct some oversights.

2
Notation and terminology

For
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let
[\ell]\!=\!\{1,2,\ldots,\ell\}
.
We define the
arithmetic genus

g
of a closed, possibly nodal and disconnected,
Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})
by

g\!-\!1=\sum_{i=1}^{m}(g_{i}\!-\!1)

if
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}
are the arithmetic genera of the topological components of
\Sigma
.
A
symmetric Riemann surface

(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is a closed, possibly nodal and disconnected,
Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})
with an anti-holomorphic involution
\sigma
.
For example, there are two topological types of such involutions on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
:

\tau,\eta\!:\mathbb{P}^{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1},\qquad\tau(z)=\frac{1}{\bar{z}},\quad\eta(z)=-\frac{1}{\bar{z}}\,.

(2.1)

If
\phi
is an involution on another space
X
,
a map
u\!:\!\Sigma\!\longrightarrow\!X
is
(\phi,\sigma)
-real

(or
\phi
-real
, or just
real
) if
u\!\circ\!\sigma\!=\!\phi\!\circ\!u
.
For
g\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
and
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, we denote by
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}

the Deligne-Mumford moduli space of stable closed, possibly nodal and disconnected,
symmetric Riemann surfaces
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
of arithmetic genus
g

with
\ell
conjugate pairs
(z_{i}^{+},z_{i}^{-})
of marked points.
This space is a smooth compact orbifold of dimension
3(g\!-\!1)\!+\!2\ell
;
it is empty if
g\!+\!\ell\!\leq\!1
.
Let
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}\!\subset\!\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}
be
the topological component parametrizing connected marked curves.
If in addition
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
, and
B
are as in Theorem
1.2
, we denote by

\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}(B;J)\subset\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

the subspace of maps from connected domains.

Let
(\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})
be a closed, but possibly nodal and disconnected, Riemann surface.
For
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, we denote by
k\bar{\partial}_{\Sigma}
the standard CR-operator
on the complex vector bundle
\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{k}
over
\Sigma
.
If
V
is any complex vector bundle over
\Sigma
and

D_{V}\!:\Gamma(\Sigma;V)\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma;V)\!\equiv\!\Gamma\big(\Sigma;(T^{*}\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})^{0,1}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!V\big)

is a
generalized Cauchy-Riemann
(or
CR-
)
operator
on
V

as in
[
28
, Section 2.2]
,
the
determinant line

\det D_{V}\equiv\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}(\ker D_{V})\otimes\big(\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}(\text{cok}\,D_{V})\big)^{*}

of
D_{V}
has a canonical
complex
orientation;
see
[
24
, Appendix A.2]
and Proposition 5.9 in the 5th arXiv version of
[
29
]
.
The space of generalized CR-operators on a complex vector bundle
V
is affine.
By
[
29
]
, the determinant lines of these operators form a line bundle
over this space;
its topology depends on the coherent system of determinant line bundles used.

If
(\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is a disjoint union of closed Riemann surfaces
(\Sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})

and
V_{i}\!=\!V|_{\Sigma_{i}}
for
i\!=\!1,2
, then
D_{V}\!=\!D_{V_{1}}\!\oplus\!D_{V_{2}}

for some generalized CR-operators
D_{V_{1}}
and
D_{V_{2}}
on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, respectively.
By the
Direct Sum
property in Section 2.2 of the 5th arXiv version of
[
29
]
,
this decomposition induces an isomorphism

\det D_{V}\approx\big(\!\det D_{V_{1}}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{V_{2}}\big)

(2.2)

between the corresponding determinant lines which depends continuously
on
D_{V_{1}}
and
D_{V_{2}}
.
If instead
V\!=\!V_{1}\!\oplus\!V_{2}
for some complex vector bundles
V_{1},V_{2}

over
\Sigma
and
D_{V}\!=\!D_{V_{1}}\!\oplus\!D_{V_{2}}

for some generalized CR-operators
D_{V_{1}}
and
D_{V_{2}}
on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
, respectively,
we similarly obtain an isomorphism (
2.2
) which depends continuously
on
D_{V_{1}}
and
D_{V_{2}}
.
However, the isomorphism in (
2.2
) in either case depends
on the coherent system of determinant line bundles used,
unless
D_{V_{1}}
and
D_{V_{2}}
are either (
\mathbb{C}
-linear) CR-operators or surjective.
By the continuous dependence, there is a canonical homotopy class
of isomorphisms (
2.2
) in both cases even if
D_{V}\!\neq\!D_{V_{1}}\!\oplus\!D_{V_{2}}
.
Since the isomorphism (
2.2
) respects the complex orientations
if
D_{V}\!=\!D_{V_{1}}\!\oplus\!D_{V_{2}}
and
D_{V_{1}},D_{V_{2}}
are CR-operators,
this is also the case for all generalized CR-operators
D_{V},D_{V_{1}},D_{V_{2}}

on
V,V_{1},V_{2}
, respectively.

Let
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
be a symmetric Riemann surface.
For
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, we denote by
k\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}
the standard CR-operator
on the real bundle pair
(\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{k},\sigma\!\times\!\mathfrak{c})
;
this is the restriction of
k\bar{\partial}_{\Sigma}
to the subspace of
\sigma
-invariant sections
with the range restricted to the subspace of
\sigma
-invariant
(0,1)
-forms.
If
(V,\varphi)
is a real bundle pair over a symmetric Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\sigma)
and

\begin{split}D_{(V,\varphi)}\!:\Gamma(\Sigma;V)^{\varphi}\equiv&\big\{\xi\!\in\!\Gamma(\Sigma;V)\!:\,\xi\!\circ\!\sigma\!=\!\varphi\!\circ\!\xi\big\}\\
&\qquad\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma;V)^{\varphi}\equiv\big\{\zeta\!\in\!\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma;V)\!:\,\zeta\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\sigma=\varphi\!\circ\!\zeta\big\}\end{split}

is a
real CR-operator

on
(V,\varphi)
as in
[
18
, Section 2.2]
, let

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\equiv\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}(\ker D_{(V,\varphi)})\otimes\big(\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}(\text{cok}\,D_{(V,\varphi)})\big)^{*}

be the
determinant
line of
D_{(V,\varphi)}
.
The space of real CR-operators on a real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)
is affine.
By
[
29
]
, the determinant lines of these operators form a line bundle
over this space.
Thus, an orientation on the determinant line of one real CR-operator on
(V,\varphi)
determines
an orientation of the determinant lines for all other such operators.

If
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is a disjoint union of symmetric Riemann surfaces
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})

and
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
and
(V_{i},\varphi_{i})\!=\!(V,\varphi)|_{\Sigma_{i}}

for
i\!=\!1,2
, then

D_{(V,\varphi)}=D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}\!\oplus\!D_{(V_{2},\varphi_{2})}

(2.3)

for some real CR-operators
D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}
and
D_{(V_{2},\varphi_{2})}
on
(V_{1},\varphi_{1})

and
(V_{2},\varphi_{2})
, respectively.
By the
Direct Sum
property for determinant line bundles, this decomposition induces an isomorphism

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\big(\!\det D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(V_{2},\varphi_{2})}\big)

(2.4)

between the corresponding determinant lines which depends continuously
on
D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}
and
D_{(V_{2},\varphi_{2})}
.
If instead

(V,\varphi)=(V_{1},\varphi_{1})\!\oplus\!(V_{2},\varphi_{2})

(2.5)

for some real bundle pairs
(V_{1},\varphi_{1}),(V_{2},\varphi_{2})
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
and
(
2.3
) holds for some real CR-operators
D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}

and
D_{(V_{2},\varphi_{2})}
on
(V_{1},\varphi_{1})
and
(V_{2},\varphi_{2})
, respectively,
we similarly obtain an isomorphism (
2.4
) which depends continuously
on
D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}
and
D_{(V_{2},\varphi_{2})}
.
There is again a canonical homotopy class of isomorphisms (
2.4
) in both cases
even if (
2.3
) does not hold.

3
Comparisons of orientations

3.1
Distinguished orientations

Suppose
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is a symmetric Riemann surface of arithmetic genus
g

and
L
is a complex vector bundle over
\Sigma
.
If
D_{L}
is a generalized CR-operator on
L
, then

\displaystyle D_{\sigma^{*}\overline{L}}\!:\Gamma(\Sigma;\sigma^{*}\overline{L})\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}\big(\Sigma;\sigma^{*}\overline{L}\big),\quad D_{\sigma^{*}\overline{L}}(\xi)=D\xi\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\sigma,\quad\hbox{and}

(3.1)

\displaystyle D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}\!\equiv\!D_{L}\!\oplus\!D_{\sigma^{*}\overline{L}}\!:\Gamma\big(\Sigma;L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\sigma_{L}^{\oplus}}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}\big(\Sigma;L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\sigma_{L}^{\oplus}}

(3.2)

are a generalized CR-operator on
\sigma^{*}\overline{L}

and a real CR-operator on
(L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})
, respectively.
The homomorphisms

\Gamma(\Sigma;L)\longrightarrow\Gamma(\Sigma;\sigma^{*}\overline{L}),~~\xi\longrightarrow\xi\!\circ\!\sigma,\quad\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}\big(\Sigma;L)\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma;\sigma^{*}\overline{L}),~~\zeta\longrightarrow\zeta\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\sigma,

are isomorphisms and determine an isomorphism

\det D_{L}\approx\det D_{\sigma^{*}\overline{L}}

(3.3)

which depends continuously on
D_{L}
.
By the continuous dependence, there is a canonical homotopy class
of isomorphisms (
3.3
) even if
D_{L}
and
D_{\sigma^{*}\overline{L}}

are not related by (
3.1
).
By the stabilization to the surjective case as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
below,
this homotopy class respects the complex orientations of the two determinant lines
if and only if

(1\!-\!g)\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L\in 2\mathbb{Z}\,,

(3.4)

as is immediate if
D_{L}
is a (
\mathbb{C}
-linear) CR-operator;
the expression on the left-hand side above is the complex index
of a (
\mathbb{C}
-linear) CR-operator on
L
or
\sigma^{*}\overline{L}
.

Furthermore, the projections

\displaystyle\Gamma\big(\Sigma;L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\sigma_{L}^{\oplus}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Gamma(\Sigma;L),

\displaystyle\quad(\xi,\xi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\xi,

(3.5)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}\big(\Sigma;L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L}\big)^{\sigma_{L}^{\oplus}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma;L),

\displaystyle\quad(\zeta,\zeta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\zeta,

are isomorphisms and determine an isomorphism

\det D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}\approx\det D_{L}

(3.6)

which depends continuously on
D_{L}
.
There is a canonical homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.6
) even
if
D_{L}
and
D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}

are not related by (
3.2
).
Via this homotopy class, the complex orientation on
\det D_{L}
induces
an orientation on
\det D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}
,
which we call the
projection orientation
of the latter;
it varies continuously with
D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}
.

For any real bundle pair
(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
over
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
,
(
2.4
) implies that the real line

\det D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\approx\big(\!\det D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\big)

has a
canonical orientation
;
it is obtained by taking the same orientation on each of the two factors
on the right-hand side above.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
be a real bundle pair over a symmetric Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})

of arithmetic genus
g
.
The homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\approx\det D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}

induced by the isomorphism
\Phi_{L}
of real bundle pairs as in (
1.6
)
respects the canonical orientation on the left-hand side and
projection orientation on the right-hand side if and only if

\frac{\big(\!(1\!-\!g)\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L\big)\!\big(\!(1\!-\!g)\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L\!-\!1\big)}{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

By the continuity of the canonical and projection orientations,
it is sufficient to establish the claim for any pair of real CR-operators on
2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})

and
(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})
.
We assume that
D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}
is two copies of
the restriction
D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}
of a CR-operator
D_{L}
on
L

to the subspaces of the domain and target invariant under the conjugation induced by
\widetilde{\sigma}

and
D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}
is given by (
3.2
).

Suppose first that the CR-operator
D_{L}
on
L
is surjective and
thus

k\equiv\dim\ker D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\ker D_{L}=\textnormal{ind}_{\mathbb{C}}D_{L}=(1\!-\!g)\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L\,.

If
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}
is a basis for
\ker D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}
,
then

(\xi_{1},0),\ldots,(\xi_{k},0),(0,\xi_{1}),\ldots,(0,\xi_{k})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\Phi_{L}(\xi_{1},0),\Phi_{L}(0,\xi_{1}),\ldots,\Phi_{L}(\xi_{k},0),\Phi_{L}(0,\xi_{k})

are a basis for
\ker D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}
determining the canonical orientation
and a basis for
\ker D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}
determining
the projection orientation.
This establishes the claim if
D_{L}
is surjective.

If
(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
is the direct sum of real bundle pairs
(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})
and
(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})

over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
and
D_{L}\!=\!D_{L_{1}}\!\oplus\!D_{L_{2}}
,
the isomorphisms
\Phi_{L},\Phi_{L_{1}},\Phi_{L_{2}}
as in (
1.6
) induce an isomorphism

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 204.01126pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 294.85028pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 294.85028pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

(3.7)

of exact triples of real CR-operators.
Since direct sums of complex vector bundles commute with the projections as in (
3.5
)
and the corresponding isomorphisms as in (
2.2
) respect the complex orientations,
the isomorphism

\det D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}\approx\big(\!\det D_{(L_{1}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L_{1}},\sigma_{L_{1}}^{\oplus})}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(L_{2}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L_{2}},\sigma_{L_{2}}^{\oplus})}\big)

(3.8)

induced by the bottom row in (
3.7
) respects the projection orientations.
By the
Exact Squares
property of determinant line bundles as in Section 2.2 in
the 5th arXiv version of
[
29
]
,
the isomorphism

\det D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\approx\big(\!\det D_{2(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{2(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})}\big)

(3.9)

induced by the top row in (
3.7
) respects the canonical orientations
if and only if

\big(\textnormal{ind}\,D_{(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})}\big)\big(\textnormal{ind}\,D_{(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})}\big)\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

Since
\textnormal{ind}\,D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\!=\!\textnormal{ind}\,D_{(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})}\!+\!\textnormal{ind}\,D_{(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})}
,
the claim holds for
(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})
if it holds for
(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
and
(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})
.

For any real bundle pair
(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})
on
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
we can find a real bundle pair
(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})
on
(\Sigma,\sigma)

so that the complex vector bundles
L_{2}
and
L\!\equiv\!L_{1}\!\oplus\!L_{2}

admit surjective CR-operators.
The claim of this lemma then holds for the real bundle pairs
(L_{2},\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})

and
(L,\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}\!\oplus\!\widetilde{\sigma}_{2})
and thus for
(L_{1},\widetilde{\sigma}_{1})
.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Suppose
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
are symmetric Riemann surfaces
of arithmetic genera
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
, respectively,

(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is the disjoint union of
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
,
and
L
is a complex vector bundle over
\Sigma
.
If
L_{i}\!=\!L|_{\Sigma_{i}}
for
i\!=\!1,2
, then
the associated homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.8
)
respects the projection orientations on the three determinant lines.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\!=\!\widetilde{\sigma}|_{L_{i}}

for
i\!=\!1,2
, then the associated homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.9
)
respects the canonical orientations on the three determinant lines if and only if

\big(\!(1\!-\!g_{1})\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L_{1}\big)\!\big(\!(1\!-\!g_{2})\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L_{2}\big)\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the homotopy class of isomorphisms (
2.2
)
with
V\!=\!L
respecting the complex orientation.
The second claim follows from the
Exact Squares
property of determinant line bundles
as in Section 2.2 in the 5th arXiv version of
[
29
]
, since

\textnormal{ind}\,D_{(V_{i},\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}=(1\!-\!g_{i})\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L_{i}

for
i\!=\!1,2
.
This claim also follows from the first claim and Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Suppose
\sigma
is an anti-holomorphic diffeomorphism between
closed Riemann surfaces
(\Sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
of arithmetic genus
g
each, so that

(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})\equiv\big(\Sigma_{1}\!\sqcup\!\Sigma_{2},\sigma,\mathfrak{j}_{1}\!\sqcup\!\mathfrak{j}_{2}\big)

(3.10)

is a symmetric Riemann surface of arithmetic genus
2g\!-\!1
,
and
(V,\varphi)
is a real bundle pair over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
.
Let
V_{i}\!=\!V|_{\Sigma_{i}}
for
i\!=\!1,2
.
The restrictions

\Gamma(\Sigma;V\big)^{\varphi}\longrightarrow\Gamma(\Sigma_{1};V_{1}),~~\xi\longrightarrow\xi|_{\Sigma_{1}},\quad\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma;V)^{\varphi}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{j}}^{0,1}(\Sigma_{1};V_{1}),~~\zeta\longrightarrow\zeta|_{\Sigma_{1}},

(3.11)

then induce isomorphisms between real CR-operators on
(V,\varphi)

and generalized CR-operators on
V_{1}
and thus an isomorphism

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\det\!\big(D_{(V,\varphi)}|_{\Sigma_{1}}\big)

(3.12)

which depends continuously on
D_{(V,\varphi)}
.
The
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!=\!1
case of the first claim below is
[
10
, Lemma 3.2]
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Suppose
(\Sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
,
(\Sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
,
g
, and
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
are as above
and
L
is a complex line bundle over
\Sigma
.
Let
\sigma_{i}\!=\!\sigma|_{\Sigma_{i}}
and
L_{i}\!=\!L|_{\Sigma_{i}}
for
i\!=\!1,2
.
The homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}\approx\det D_{L_{1}\oplus\sigma_{1}^{*}\overline{L_{2}}}

(3.13)

as in (
3.12
)
respects the projection orientation on the left-hand side and
the complex orientation on the right-hand side if and only if

(1\!-\!g)\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!+\!\deg L_{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\,.

If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
,
then the homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\approx\det D_{2L_{1}}

(3.14)

as in (
3.12
) respects the canonical orientation on the left-hand side and
the complex orientation on the right-hand side.

Proof.

Since the restrictions to
\Sigma_{1}
as in (
3.11
) commute with direct sums,
the second claim follows immediately from the definition of the canonical orientation
on the left-hand side of (
3.14
).
The first claim follows from the sentence containing (
3.4
) with
L
replaced by
L_{2}
,
since
D_{L}\!=\!D_{L_{1}}\!\oplus\!D_{L_{2}}
for some generalized CR-operators

D_{L_{1}},D_{L_{2}}
on
L_{1},L_{2}
, respectively.
If
L
admits a conjugation
\widetilde{\sigma}
lifting
\sigma
,
then this claim also follows from the second claim and Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Suppose
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is a symmetric Riemann surface with a conjugate pair
of nodes
\{x^{+},x^{-}\}
.
Let
(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\mathfrak{j}})
be the symmetric Riemann surface obtained from
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})

by replacing
x^{+}
and
x^{-}
by 2 smooth points each.
We denote by
q\!:\widehat{\Sigma}\!\longrightarrow\!\Sigma

the holomorphic projection and by
\widehat{x}_{1}^{\pm},\widehat{x}_{2}^{\pm}\!\in\!\widehat{\Sigma}

the preimages of
x^{\pm}
so that
\widehat{\sigma}(\widehat{x}_{i}^{\pm})\!=\!\widehat{x}_{i}^{\mp}

for
i\!=\!1,2
.
We will then call the tuple

\big(q\!:\widehat{\Sigma}\!\longrightarrow\!\Sigma,\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\mathfrak{j}},(\widehat{x}_{1}^{+},\widehat{x}_{1}^{-}),(\widehat{x}_{2}^{+},\widehat{x}_{2}^{-})\!\big)

(3.15)

the normalization
of
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
at
\{x^{+},x^{-}\}
.

Let
(V,\varphi)
be a real bundle pair over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
. Define

\displaystyle\textnormal{ev}_{x}^{+}\!:\Gamma(\widehat{\Sigma},q^{*}V)^{q^{*}\varphi}\longrightarrow V_{x^{+}},~~\textnormal{ev}_{x}^{+}(\xi)=\xi(\widehat{x}_{1}^{+})\!-\!\xi(\widehat{x}_{2}^{+}),

\displaystyle\textnormal{ev}_{x}^{+}\!:\Gamma^{0,1}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{j}}}(\widehat{\Sigma},q^{*}V)^{q^{*}\varphi}\longrightarrow\{0\},\qquad\mathbf{0}_{V_{x^{+}}}\!:V_{x^{+}}\longrightarrow\{0\}\,.

A real CR-operator
D_{(V,\varphi)}
on
(V,\varphi)
lifts to
a real CR-operator
q^{*}D_{(V,\varphi)}
on
q^{*}(V,\varphi)
so that
the triple

0\longrightarrow D_{(V,\varphi)}\longrightarrow q^{*}D_{(V,\varphi)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{ev}_{x}^{+}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{0}_{V_{x^{+}}}\longrightarrow 0

of operators is exact and thus induces an isomorphism

\det q^{*}D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\big(\!\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\big)\!\otimes\!\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(V_{x^{+}}\big)

(3.16)

which depends continuously on
D_{(V,\varphi)}
;
see the
Exact Triples
property in Section 2.2 in the 5th arXiv version of
[
29
]
.
The
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!=\!1
case of the first claim below is
[
11
, Lemma 7.5]
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Suppose
L
is a complex vector bundle over a symmetric Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})

with a conjugate pair of nodes
\{x^{+},x^{-}\}
and (
3.15
)
is the normalization of
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
at
\{x^{+},x^{-}\}
.
If
D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}
is a real CR-operator on
the real bundle pair
(L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
then the associated isomorphism (
3.16
) respects
the projection orientations
on the two determinant lines and the complex orientation on the last factor
if and only if
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
and

D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}
is a real CR-operator on the real bundle pair
2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
then the associated isomorphism (
3.16
) respects the canonical orientations
on the two determinant lines and the complex orientation on the last factor.

Proof.

By the continuous dependence of (
3.16
) on
D_{(V,\varphi)}
,
it is sufficient to establish each claim for
a single real CR-operator on the relevant real bundle pair.
Since the real dimension of
L_{x^{+}}
is even,
the second claim for
D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\!=\!D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\!\oplus\!D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}

follows from the
Exact Squares
property applied
to the commutative square of operators in Figure
1
.

(3.17)

is an isomorphism of exact triples of Fredholm operators.
The bottom row respects the complex orientations of the three determinant lines,
while the left and middle vertical isomorphisms intertwine the projection orientations
on the domains with the complex orientations on the targets.
The right vertical isomorphism is orientation-preserving with respect to the complex orientations
on its domain and target if and only if the complex dimension of
L_{x^{-}}
is even.
This establishes the first claim.
∎

Suppose now that
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
is a symmetric Riemann surface with an isolated real node
x
,
called
E
-node
in
[
17
]
and elsewhere.
Let
(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\mathfrak{j}})
be the symmetric Riemann surface obtained from
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})

by replacing
x
by 2 smooth points.
We denote by
q\!:\widehat{\Sigma}\!\longrightarrow\!\Sigma

the holomorphic projection and by
\widehat{x}^{+},\widehat{x}^{-}\!\in\!\widehat{\Sigma}

the preimages of
x
; they are interchanged by the involution
\widehat{\sigma}
.
We will then call the tuple

\big(q\!:\widehat{\Sigma}\!\longrightarrow\!\Sigma,\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\mathfrak{j}},(\widehat{x}^{+},\widehat{x}^{-})\big)

(3.18)

the normalization
of
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
at
x
.

Let
(V,\varphi)
be a real bundle pair over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
.
Since
x\!\in\!\Sigma^{\sigma}
,
V_{x}^{\varphi}\!\subset\!V_{x}
is a half-dimensional totally real subspace.
An orientation on
V_{x}^{\varphi}
and the complex orientation on
V_{x}
induce
an orientation on
V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}
via the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow V_{x}^{\varphi}\longrightarrow V_{x}\longrightarrow V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}\longrightarrow 0.

If
(V,\varphi)\!=\!(L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})
for a complex vector bundle
L\!\longrightarrow\!X
,
we call the orientation on
V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}
induced by the associated projection orientation
of
V_{x}^{\varphi}
the
projection orientation
.
If
(V,\varphi)\!=\!2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
for some real bundle pair
(L,\widetilde{\sigma})

over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
, then
V_{x}^{\varphi}\!=\!2L_{x}^{\widetilde{\sigma}}
.
We call the orientation on
V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}
induced by the associated canonical orientation
of
2L_{x}^{\widetilde{\sigma}}
the
canonical orientation
.
The isomorphism (
1.7
) with
\phi\!=\!\sigma

intertwines the canonical orientation on its domain
and the projection orientation on its target if and only if
\binom{\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L-1}{2}
is even.
The same applies to the isomorphism induced by (
1.6
)
on the corresponding quotients
V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}
.
Thus, Lemma
3.5
below is consistent with Lemma
3.1
.

Define

\displaystyle\textnormal{ev}_{x}\!:\Gamma(\widehat{\Sigma},q^{*}V)^{q^{*}\varphi}\longrightarrow V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi},~~\textnormal{ev}_{x}(\xi)=\xi(\widehat{x}^{+})\!+\!V_{x}^{\varphi},

\displaystyle\textnormal{ev}_{x}\!:\Gamma^{0,1}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{j}}}(\widehat{\Sigma},q^{*}V)^{q^{*}\varphi}\longrightarrow\{0\},\qquad\mathbf{0}_{V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}}\!:V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}\longrightarrow\{0\}\,.

A real CR-operator
D_{(V,\varphi)}
on
(V,\varphi)
lifts to
a real CR-operator
q^{*}D_{(V,\varphi)}
on
q^{*}(V,\varphi)
so that
the triple

0\longrightarrow D_{(V,\varphi)}\longrightarrow q^{*}D_{(V,\varphi)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{ev}_{x}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{0}_{V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}}\longrightarrow 0

of operators is exact and thus induces an isomorphism

\det q^{*}D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\big(\!\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\big)\!\otimes\!\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(V_{x}/V_{x}^{\varphi}\big)

(3.19)

which depends continuously on
D_{(V,\varphi)}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Suppose
L
is a complex vector bundle over a symmetric Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})

with an
E
-node
x
and arithmetic genus
g
.
Let (
3.18
) be the normalization of
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
at
x
.
If
D_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})}
is a real CR-operator on
the real bundle pair
(L\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L},\sigma_{L}^{\oplus})
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
then the associated isomorphism (
3.19
) respects the projection orientations
on the three lines if and only if
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
and

D_{2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}
is a real CR-operator on the real bundle pair
2(L,\widetilde{\sigma})
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
then the associated isomorphism (
3.19
) respects the canonical orientations
on the three lines if and only if
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L(g\!+\!\deg L)
is even.

Proof.

For the purposes of the second claim, we replace
L_{x^{+}}
by
L_{x}/L_{x}^{\widetilde{\sigma}}

in the exact square of Fredholm operators in Figure
1
.
The canonical orientation on
(2L)/(2L)^{2\widetilde{\sigma}}
as defined above is
the same as the canonical orientation with respect to the isomorphism

(2L)/(2L)^{2\widetilde{\sigma}}\!\approx\!2(L/L)^{\widetilde{\sigma}}
if and only if
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L
is even.
By the Exact Squares property, the middle row in the diagram in Figure
1

respects the canonical orientations on the two determinant lines and
the latter orientation on
(2L)/(2L)^{2\widetilde{\sigma}}
if and only if

\big(\textnormal{ind}\,\mathbf{0}_{L_{x}/L_{x}^{\varphi}}\big)\big(\textnormal{ind},D_{(L,\widetilde{\sigma})}\big)=\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\big((1\!-\!g)\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\!+\!\deg L\big)

is even. Combining the two statements, we obtain the second claim.

For the purposes of the first claim, we define

\textnormal{ev}_{x}\!:\Gamma(\widehat{\Sigma},q^{*}L)\longrightarrow L_{x},~~\textnormal{ev}_{x}(\xi)=\xi(\widehat{x}^{+})\!-\!\xi(\widehat{x}^{-})\quad\textnormal{ev}_{x}\!:\Gamma^{0,1}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{j}}}(\widehat{\Sigma},q^{*}L)\longrightarrow\{0\}.

With the assumptions as in (
3.17
), the diagram

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{0}_{(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L})_{x}/(L\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L})_{x}^{\sigma_{L}^{\oplus}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 253.65887pt\raise-15.69293pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\approx}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 253.65887pt\raise-24.44142pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 325.05313pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.06458pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.06458pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.73682pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 132.73682pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{q^{*}D_{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 191.58098pt\raise-26.37892pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textnormal{ev}_{x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 242.56165pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 242.56165pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{0}_{L_{x}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 325.05313pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 325.05313pt\raise-31.38586pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

is an isomorphism of exact triples of Fredholm operators.
The bottom row respects the complex orientations of the three determinant lines, as before,
while the left and middle vertical isomorphisms intertwine the projection orientations
on the domains with the complex orientations on the targets.
The right vertical isomorphism, which is given by taking the difference of the two components
of
L_{x}\!\oplus\!L_{x}
, is orientation-preserving with respect to the projection orientation
on its domain and the complex orientation on its target
if and only if the complex dimension of
L_{x}
is even.
This establishes the first claim.
∎

3.2
Induced orientations

Let
(\Sigma,\sigma)
be a symmetric Riemann surface and

D_{(V,\varphi)}
be a real CR-operator on a rank
k
real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
.
A real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
for
(V,\varphi)
determines
a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\begin{split}\big(\!\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(L^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\sigma_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})}\big)&\approx\det D_{(V\oplus L^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\varphi\oplus\sigma_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})}\\
&\approx\det\!\big(\!(k\!+\!2)\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\approx\det\!\big(k\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\!\otimes\!\det\!\big(2\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\end{split}

(3.20)

via isomorphisms for direct sums of real bundle pairs as in (
2.4
)
and the homotopy class of isomorphisms (
1.4
).
Combined with the projection orientation on the second determinant line above
and the canonical orientation on the last determinant line,
(
3.20
) determines a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\det\!\big(k\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\approx\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)^{\otimes k}

(3.21)

of real lines.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
, isomorphisms as in (
2.4
)
and the homotopy class of isomorphisms (
1.8
)
determine a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\begin{split}\big(\!\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det\,D_{2(L^{*},\widetilde{\sigma}^{*})}\big)&\approx\det D_{(V\oplus 2L^{*},\varphi\oplus 2\widetilde{\sigma}^{*})}\\
&\approx\det\!\big(\!(k\!+\!2)\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\approx\det\!\big(k\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\!\otimes\!\det\!\big(2\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\,.\end{split}

(3.22)

Along with the canonical orientations on the second and last determinant lines above,
(
3.22
) determines another homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.21
).

The next four corollaries follow readily from the corresponding lemmas.
The
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}V\!=\!1
case of the first claim of Corollary
3.8
is
[
11
, Lemma 7.5]
.
The second claims of Corollaries
3.7
and
3.8

are Lemma 3.1 and Corollary 4.5, respectively, in
[
18
]
.

Corollary 3.6

(of Lemma
3.2
)
.

Suppose
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
,
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
,
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
,
and
g_{1},g_{2}
are as in Lemma
3.2
,

(V,\varphi)
is a rank
k
real bundle pair over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
,
and
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
is a real orientation for

.
If

(V_{i},\varphi_{i})=(V,\varphi)\big|_{\Sigma_{i}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\big(L_{i},[\psi_{i}],\mathfrak{s}_{i}\big)=\big(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s}\big)\!\big|_{\Sigma_{i}}

for

,
then the diagram

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern (3.23)

with the homotopy classes of the horizontal isomorphisms
determined by the real orientations
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
and
(L_{i},[\psi_{i}],\mathfrak{s}_{i})
,
commutes if and only if
(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma

and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\!=\!\widetilde{\sigma}|_{L_{i}}
for
i\!=\!1,2
,
then the diagram (
3.23
) with (
3.20
) replaced by (
3.22
)
commutes if and only if

(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)\!+\!\big(g_{1}\!-\!1\!+\!\deg L_{1}\big)\!\big(g_{2}\!-\!1\!+\!\deg L_{2}\big)\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

Since the indices of
D_{(L_{i}^{*}\oplus\sigma_{i}^{*}\overline{L}_{i}^{*},\sigma_{L_{i}^{*}}^{\oplus})}
,

D_{2(L_{i}^{*},\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{*})}
, and
2\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma_{i},\sigma_{i})}
are even,
the outer isomorphisms in (
3.20
) and (
3.22
)
commute with the isomorphisms (
2.4
) associated with
the disjoint union of
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
.
Since the restrictions of the homotopy classes of
isomorphisms (
1.4
) and (
1.8
) determined by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
to
\Sigma_{i}

are determined by
(L_{i},[\psi_{i}],\mathfrak{s}_{i})
,
the homotopy classes of the middle isomorphisms in (
3.20
) and (
3.22
)
also commute with the isomorphisms (
2.4
) associated with
the disjoint union of
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
.
The first claim of the corollary thus follows from the first statement of Lemma
3.2

with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and the second statement of this lemma with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
The second claim of the corollary similarly follows from the second statement of
Lemma
3.2
with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Corollary 3.7

(of Lemma
3.3
)
.

Suppose
(\Sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
,
(\Sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})
,
g
,
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
, and
\sigma_{1}

are as in Lemma
3.3
.
Let
(V,\varphi)
and
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
be as in Corollary
3.6
.
If
L_{i}\!=\!L|_{\Sigma_{i}}
for
i\!=\!1,2
,
then the composition

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}|_{\Sigma_{1}}\underset{\approx}{\xleftarrow{(\ref{Dblorient_e})}}\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\underset{\approx}{\xrightarrow{(\ref{ROrientCompl_e2})}}\det\big(k\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\underset{\approx}{\xrightarrow{(\ref{Dblorient_e})}}\det\big(k\bar{\partial}_{\Sigma_{1}}\big)\,,

(3.24)

with the middle isomorphism determined by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
intertwines the complex orientations on the two ends if and only if

g\!-\!1\!+\!\deg L_{2}\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
,
then the composition (
3.24
) with (
3.20
)
replaced by (
3.22
)
intertwines the complex orientations on the two ends.

Proof.

Isomorphisms for direct sums of complex vector bundles as in (
2.2
)
and the restrictions of the isomorphisms (
1.4
) to the bundles over
\Sigma_{1}

determine a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\begin{split}\big(\!\det D_{(V,\varphi)}|_{\Sigma_{1}}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(L^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\sigma_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})}|_{\Sigma_{1}}\big)&\approx\det D_{(V\oplus L^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\varphi\oplus\sigma_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})}|_{\Sigma_{1}}\\
&\approx\det\!\big(\!(k\!+\!2)\bar{\partial}_{\Sigma}\big)\approx\det\!\big(k\bar{\partial}_{\Sigma}\big)\!\otimes\!\det\!\big(2\bar{\partial}_{\Sigma}\big).\end{split}

(3.25)

Since the restrictions of isomorphisms (
1.4
) are complex trivializations

V\!\oplus\!L^{*}\!\oplus\!\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*}|_{\Sigma_{1}}
,
(
3.25
) is orientation-preserving with respect to the complex orientations
on all determinant lines above.
The isomorphisms (
3.12
) intertwine the isomorphisms in (
3.20
)
and (
3.25
).
The first claim of the corollary thus follows from the first statement of Lemma
3.3

with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and the second statement of this lemma with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
The second claim of the corollary similarly follows from the second statement of
Lemma
3.3
with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
∎

\mathbb{C}^{k}
,
does not commute up to homotopy.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
,
then the diagram (
3.26
) with (
3.20
)
replaced by (
3.22
) commutes up to homotopy.

Proof.

Since the indices of
D_{(L^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\sigma_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})}
,

D_{2(L^{*},\widetilde{\sigma}^{*})}
, and
2\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}
are even,
the outer isomorphisms in (
3.20
) and (
3.22
)
commute with the corresponding isomorphisms (
3.16
).
Since the pullbacks of the homotopy classes of
isomorphisms (
1.4
) and (
1.8
) determined by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
to
\widehat{\Sigma}

are determined by
q^{*}(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
the homotopy classes of the middle isomorphisms in (
3.20
) and (
3.22
)
also commute with the corresponding isomorphisms (
3.16
).
The first claim of the corollary thus follows from the first statement of Lemma
3.4

with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and the second statement of this lemma with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
The second claim of the corollary similarly follows from the second statement of
Lemma
3.4
with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Corollary 3.9

(of Lemma
3.5
)
.

(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
,

,

,

, and

(3.27)

with the homotopy classes of the horizontal isomorphisms determined
by the real orientations
q^{*}(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
and
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
the induced orientation of
V_{x}^{\varphi}
, and
the standard orientation of
\mathbb{C}^{k}/\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
commutes up to homotopy if and only if
g\!-\!1\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\sigma}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\sigma
,
then the diagram (
3.27
) with (
3.20
)
replaced by (
3.22
) commutes up to homotopy
if and only if
\deg L\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Corollary
3.8
,
with Lemma
3.4
replaced by
3.5
.
∎

By Definition
1.1
(RO3)
,
a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
on
(V,\varphi)
determines a
relative spin structure

on the real bundle
V^{\varphi}
over
X^{\phi}\!\subset\!X
in
the spirit of
[
8
, Definition 8.1.2]
,
which we call the
the associated relative spin structure on
V^{\varphi}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi

and
L^{\widetilde{\phi}}\!\longrightarrow\!X^{\phi}
is orientable,
then
2(L^{*})^{\widetilde{\phi}^{*}}
has a canonical homotopy
class of trivializations as in the proof of
[
17
, Corollary 5.6]
.
Such a real orientation on
(V,\varphi)
thus determines a spin structure on
V^{\varphi}
,
which we call the
the associated spin structure on
V^{\varphi}
.

Let
D_{(V,\varphi)}
be a real CR-operator on a real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)
over
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)
.
By
[
23
, Lemma 6.37]
, a (relative) spin structure on
the real vector bundle
V^{\varphi}
over the
\tau
-fixed locus
S^{1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{1}

and a choice of a disk
\mathbb{D}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{1}
determine an orientation on
\det D_{(V,\varphi)}
.
If
(V,\varphi)
admits a real orientation, then
\deg V\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and
this orientation does not depend on the choice of
\mathbb{D}
.
Since the kernel of the surjective real CR-operator
\bar{\partial}_{(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)}
consists
of the constant
\mathbb{R}
-valued functions on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
,

\det\bar{\partial}_{(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)}
is canonically oriented.
A homotopy class of isomorphisms as in (
3.21
) thus also determines
an orientation on
\det D_{(V,\varphi)}
.
Any complex vector bundle
L
over
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)
admits a conjugation
\widetilde{\tau}

lifting
\tau
.
The real vector bundle
L^{\widetilde{\tau}}
is orientable if and only if
\deg L\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.

Corollary 3.10
.

Suppose
(V,\varphi)
is a real bundle pair over
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)
,

(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
is a real orientation for
(V,\varphi)
, and
D_{(V,\varphi)}
is a real CR-operator.
The orientations on
\det D_{(V,\varphi)}
induced by the associated relative spin structure
on
V^{\varphi}
and by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.20
)
are the same if and only if
\deg V\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\tau}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\tau
,
the former orientation and the orientation induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})

via (
3.22
) with
\widetilde{\sigma}\!=\!\widetilde{\tau}

are the same if and only if
\deg V\!\cong\!0,-2

​​​
\mod 8
.
If
\deg V\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
, the orientation on
\det D_{(V,\varphi)}
induced
by the associated spin structure and the latter orientation are the same.

Proof.

The third claim follows from
[
18
, Lemma 3.3]
with replaced by
L^{*}
.
The
\deg V\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
and
\deg V\!-\!2\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
cases
of the second claim follow from

[
18
, Lemma 3.4]
and
[
18
, Corollary 3.6]
, respectively,
with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
and thus with
\deg L
replaced by
-(\deg V)/2
;
they are established via technical computations.
The first claim follows from the second and Lemma
3.1

with
L
replaced by
L^{*}
.
∎

3.3
Orientations of moduli spaces

Let
g,\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
with
g\!+\!\ell\!\geq\!2
.
Denote by
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}\!\subset\!\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}
the subspace of smooth curves.
The determinants of the operators
\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}

on symmetric Riemann surfaces form a real line orbi-bundle
\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}}

over
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}
.
By
[
17
, Propositions 5.9,6.1]
, the real line orbi-bundle

\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(T(\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet})\!\big)\otimes\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}}\big)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}

(3.28)

has a natural orientation.
The construction of this orientation over the equivalence class

[\mathcal{C}]\!\in\!\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}

of a smooth marked curve
\mathcal{C}

with the underlying symmetric Riemann surface
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
involves:

(
\mathcal{M}1
)

the Kodaira-Spencer and Dolbeault isomorphisms that
identify
T_{[\mathcal{C}]}(\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet})
with the cokernel
H^{1}(\Sigma;T\mathcal{C})^{\varphi}
of
an injective real CR-operator on a real line bundle pair
(T\mathcal{C},\varphi)
over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
;

(
\mathcal{M}2
)

the Serre Duality that identifies
H^{1}(\Sigma;T\mathcal{C})^{\varphi}

with the dual
(H^{0}(\Sigma;T\mathcal{C}^{*}\!\otimes\!T^{*}\Sigma)^{\varphi*\otimes\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*}})^{*}

of the kernel
of a surjective real CR-operator
\bar{\partial}_{(T\mathcal{C}^{*}\otimes T^{*}\Sigma,\varphi*\otimes\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*})}

on the real bundle pair
(T\mathcal{C}^{*}\!\otimes\!T^{*}\Sigma,\varphi^{*}\!\otimes\!\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*})

over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
;

(
\mathcal{M}3
)

an exact triple of Fredholm operators that identifies

\det\bar{\partial}_{(T\mathcal{C}^{*}\otimes T^{*}\Sigma,\varphi*\otimes\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*})}
with
the tensor product of
\det\bar{\partial}_{(T^{*}\Sigma^{\otimes 2},\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*\otimes 2})}

and the top exterior power of a complex vector space and thus
determines an orientation on the tensor product of the two determinant lines;

(
\mathcal{M}4
)

a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det\bar{\partial}_{(T^{*}\Sigma^{\otimes 2},\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*\otimes 2})}\!\approx\!\det\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}

obtained via (
3.22
) from
a canonical real orientation on
(T^{*}\Sigma^{\otimes 2},\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*\otimes 2})

with
L\!=\!T^{*}\Sigma
and involution
\textnormal{d}\sigma^{*}
;

(
\mathcal{M}5
)

twisting the resulting homotopy class of isomorphisms

\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}(T_{[\mathcal{C}]}(\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet})\!)\!\approx\!\det\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}

by
(-1)^{(g-1)+|\sigma|}
, where
|\sigma|
is the number of connected components of
\Sigma^{\sigma}
.

The parity of each of the two summands in the twisting exponent in
(

⁢
M
5

)

is invariant under disjoint unions of symmetric surfaces and under
the first node-identifying immersion in (
3.36
) below.
This exponent is even if the domain is a doublet as in (
3.10
) or
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)
.
Thus, the twisting in
(

⁢
M
5

)
can be ignored for the purposes
of Lemma
3.11
and
Propositions
3.12
-
3.14
and
3.18
below.

Let
(X,\omega,\phi)
be a real symplectic manifold of real dimension
2n
and

J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}^{\phi}
.
We denote by

\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)\subset\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

the subspace of maps from smooth stable domains and by

\mathfrak{f}\!:\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}

the forgetful morphism dropping the map component.
The determinants of the linearizations of the
\bar{\partial}_{J}
-operators on
the space of real maps from symmetric surfaces to
(X,\phi)

form a real line orbi-bundle
\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}

over
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
; see
[
17
, Section 4.3]
.
The forgetful morphism
\mathfrak{f}
induces an isomorphism

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)\!\big)\approx\big(\!\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}\big)\!\otimes\!\mathfrak{f}^{*}\!\big(\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}(T\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet})\!\big)

(3.29)

of real line orbi-bundles over
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
;
the line orbi-bundle on the left-hand side is the top exterior power of
the virtual tangent bundle for the moduli space
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
.
A real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
on
(X,\omega,\phi)
determines a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}\approx\mathfrak{f}^{*}\!\big(\!(\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}})^{\otimes n}\big)

(3.30)

of real line orbi-bundles over
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
via (
3.20
),
with
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
replaced by
u^{*}(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
for each stable map
u

representing an element of
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
,
a homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.30
) is similarly determined
via (
3.22
).

Combining (
3.28
)-(
3.30
), we obtain a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)\!\big)\approx\mathfrak{f}^{*}\!\big(\!(\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}})^{\otimes(n+1)}\big)

(3.31)

of real line orbi-bundles over
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
.
If
n\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, we can break the right-hand side into
(n\!+\!1)/2

products of
\mathfrak{f}^{*}(\!(\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}})^{\otimes 2})
, each of which is canonically oriented,
and thus obtain an orientation on
\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\star}(B;J)
.
Via the stabilization of the domain as in the proof of
[
17
, Corollary 5.10]
,
this orientation extends over the subspace

\mathfrak{M}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\subset\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

of all maps from smooth domains.
By the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 1.3]
, the extended orientation
further extends over the rest of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
.

For
g_{1},g_{2}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let

\iota_{\sqcup}\!:\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1},\ell_{1}}^{\bullet}\!\times\!\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{2},\ell_{2}}^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1}+g_{2}-1,\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}^{\bullet}

be the open embedding obtained by taking the disjoint union of two marked curves
and re-ordering their conjugate pairs of marked points in some fixed way.
If
[\mathbf{u}_{i}]\!\in\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g_{i},\ell_{i}}^{\phi;\bullet}(B_{i};J)
for
i\!=\!1,2
, we similarly define

\big[\mathbf{u}_{1}\!\sqcup\!\mathbf{u}_{2}\big]\in\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g_{1}+g_{2}-1,\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}^{\phi;\bullet}(B_{1}\!+\!B_{2};J)

to be the equivalence class of the stable map obtained by taking the disjoint union of the two maps
and ordering the conjugate pairs of marked points of the two maps in some fixed way.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose
g_{1},g_{2}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
.
Let

be the component projections.

(3.32)

with the homotopy classes of the horizontal isomorphisms determined by
the orientations of the line orbi-bundles (

), commutes.

Proof.

Let
([\mathcal{C}_{1}],[\mathcal{C}_{2}])\!\in\!\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{1},\ell_{1}}^{\bullet}\!\times\!\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{2},\ell_{2}}^{\bullet}

, respectively, and

.
The horizontal isomorphisms in (

) factor through the horizontal isomorphisms in
the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\approx}

)

Proposition 3.12
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
n
,
J
, and
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
are as in Theorem
1.2

and
g_{i},\ell_{i}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
,

,
and
[\mathbf{u}_{i}]\!\in\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g_{i},\ell_{i}}^{\phi;\bullet}(B_{i};J)
for
i\!=\!1,2
.
The natural isomorphism

T_{[\mathbf{u}_{1}\sqcup\mathbf{u}_{2}]}\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g_{1}+g_{2}-1,\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}^{\phi;\bullet}(B_{1}\!+\!B_{2};J)\!\big)\approx T_{[\mathbf{u}_{1}]}\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g_{1},\ell_{1}}^{\phi;\bullet}(B_{1};J)\!\big)\!\oplus\!T_{[\mathbf{u}_{2}]}\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g_{2},\ell_{2}}^{\phi;\bullet}(B_{2};J)\!\big)

(3.33)

is orientation-preserving with respect to the orientations induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})

if and only if

(n\!-\!1)(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)/2\in 2\mathbb{Z}.

If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
and
the homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.30
) is obtained
via (
3.22
), then this isomorphism is orientation-preserving if and only if

(n\!-\!1)(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)/2\!+\!\big(g_{1}\!-\!1\!+\!\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B_{1}\rangle/2\big)\big(g_{2}\!-\!1\!+\!\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B_{2}\rangle/2\big)\in 2\mathbb{Z}\,.

Proof.

The second claim follows from the first and Lemma
3.1
applied three times.
It can be assumed that the domains
(\Sigma_{1},\sigma_{1},\mathfrak{j}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2},\mathfrak{j}_{2})

of
[\mathbf{u}_{1}]
and
[\mathbf{u}_{2}]
, respectively, are smooth and stable with their markings
and thus so is their disjoint union
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})

Let
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\mathcal{C}
be the marked domains of
\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2},\mathbf{u}_{1}\!\sqcup\!\mathbf{u}_{2}
, respectively,
and
u_{1},u_{2},u
their map components.
We denote the top exterior powers of the relevant tangent spaces
of
[\mathcal{C}_{i}],[\mathcal{C}]
and
[\mathbf{u}_{i}],[\mathbf{u}_{1}\!\sqcup\!\mathbf{u}_{2}]
by
\mathcal{M}_{i},\mathcal{M}
and
\mathfrak{M}_{i},\mathfrak{M}
, respectively, and
the determinants of the real CR-operators
D_{u_{i}^{*}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)},D_{u^{*}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}

and
\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma_{i},\sigma_{i})},\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}
by
D_{i},D
and
\bar{\partial}_{i},\bar{\partial}
, respectively.

\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{RfMisom_e})}
\textstyle{D\!\otimes\!\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{Runionorient_e0})}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{reldetdfn_e0})}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{RcMisom_e})}

\scriptstyle{(\ref{reldetdfn_e0})}

Figure 2:
Isomorphisms inducing the orientations of Proposition
3.12

The tangent spaces in the statement of the proposition are oriented by the top
and bottom rows of the top diagram in Figure
2
from

(
\mathfrak{o}1
)

the canonical orientations on the
(n\!+\!1)/2
factors of

\bar{\partial}^{\otimes 2},\bar{\partial}_{i}^{\otimes 2}
,

(
\mathfrak{o}2
)

the projection (resp. canonical) orientations on the determinants of real CR-operators
on the real bundle pairs

u^{*}(L^{*}\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\phi_{L^{*}}^{\oplus}),u_{i}^{*}(L^{*}\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}^{*},\phi_{L^{*}}^{\oplus})
in the first
(resp.
2u^{*}(L^{*},\widetilde{\phi}^{*}),2u_{i}^{*}(L^{*},\widetilde{\phi}^{*})

in the second) claim of the proposition.

The vertical isomorphisms at the bottom of this diagram interchange the middle factors
in their domains and multiply the result by
(-1)
to the power of the index
of
D_{2}
times the dimension of
\mathcal{M}_{1}
.
Since
n\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and
\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B_{2}\rangle\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
,

\begin{split}\big(\textnormal{ind}\,D_{2}\big)\big(\!\dim\mathcal{M}_{1}\big)&\cong(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)\\
&\cong\big(\textnormal{ind}\,n\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma_{2},\sigma_{2})}\big)\!\big(\textnormal{ind}\,\bar{\partial}_{1}\big)\mod 2\end{split}

The two rectangles with solid arrows in this diagram commute.

The isomorphisms in the upper right rectangle in the top diagram are the tensor products
of the isomorphisms in the bottom diagram and in (
3.32
).
For the purposes of (
2.4
),

\bar{\partial}^{\otimes n}
is viewed as the determinant of the CR-operator
n\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}

on
(\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{n},\sigma\!\times\!\mathfrak{c})
in these diagrams.
By Corollary
3.6
, the bottom diagram would commute up to homotopy
if the dashed arrow were multiplied by
(-1)
to the power of

(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)\quad\Big(\hbox{resp.~}(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)\!+\!\big(g_{1}\!-\!1\!+\!\langle c_{1}(L),B_{1}\rangle\!\big)\!\big(g_{2}\!-\!1\!+\!\langle c_{1}(L),B_{2}\rangle\!\big)\!\Big)

(3.34)

in the first (resp. second) claim of the proposition.
Along with Lemma
3.11
, this implies that the upper right rectangle in the top diagram
would commute up to homotopy if the dashed arrow were multiplied by
(-1)

to the power of (
3.34
).

The pullback of the canonical orientation on
\bar{\partial}_{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\!\otimes\!\bar{\partial}_{2}^{\otimes(n+1)}

by the bottom right vertical arrow in the top diagram (with the specified sign)
and the top right vertical arrow (without any sign) differs from
the canonical orientation on
\bar{\partial}^{\otimes(n+1)}
by
(-1)
to the power of

\binom{n\!+\!1}{2}\big(\textnormal{ind}\,\bar{\partial}_{1}\big)\big(\textnormal{ind}\,\bar{\partial}_{2}\big)\cong(n\!+\!1)(g_{1}\!-\!1)(g_{2}\!-\!1)/2\mod 2.

Combining this with the correcting sign (
3.34
) for
the top right vertical arrow, we obtain the claim.
∎

For
g\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
,
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,
B\!\in\!H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
, and
J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}
,
we denote by

the moduli space of stable

-holomorphic degree
B
maps
from closed, possibly nodal and disconnected, Riemann surfaces of arithmetic genus
g

with
\ell
marked points.
If in addition
[\ell]\!=\!S^{+}\!\sqcup\!S^{-}
, let

\iota_{g;S^{+},S^{-}}\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{2g-1,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B\!-\!\phi_{*}(B);J\big)

be the open immersion sending the equivalence class of a stable map
\mathbf{u}

with the domain
(\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})
and map component
u
to the equivalence class of
the map

u\!\sqcup\!\{\phi\!\circ\!u\}\!:\big(\Sigma\!\sqcup\!\Sigma,\mathfrak{j}\!\sqcup\!(-\mathfrak{j})\!\big)\longrightarrow(X,J)

with the involution
\sigma
interchanging the two copies of
\Sigma

and the first copy of
\Sigma

with
i\!\in\!S^{+}

and
z_{i}^{-}
