i\!\in\!S^{-}
at the positions of the corresponding marked points
z_{i}

of
\mathbf{u}
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
,
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
g
,
\ell
, and
B
are as in Theorem
1.2
,

[\ell]\!=\!S^{+}\!\sqcup\!S^{-}
, and
[\mathbf{u}]\!\in\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}(B;J)
.
The isomorphism

\textnormal{d}_{[\mathbf{u}]}\iota_{g;S^{+},S^{-}}\!:T_{[\mathbf{u}]}\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}(B;J)\!\big)\longrightarrow T_{\iota_{g;S^{+},S^{-}}([\mathbf{u}])}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{2g-1,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}\big(B\!-\!\phi_{*}(B);J\big)

is orientation-preserving with respect to the orientation on the target induced
by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.20
) and the complex orientation
on the domain if and only if
\langle c_{1}(L),\phi_{*}(B)\rangle\!+\!|S^{-}|\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
and
the homotopy class of isomorphisms (
3.30
) is obtained
via (
3.22
), then this isomorphism is orientation-preserving if and only if

(g\!-\!1)\!+\!|S^{-}|\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

The second claim is
[
18
, Theorem 1.4]
;
it also follows from the first claim and Lemma
3.1

with
g
,
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}\!L
, and
\deg L
replaced by
2g\!-\!1
, 1, and
-\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\rangle
,
respectively.
The first claim stated for certain one-dimensional targets
(X,\omega,\phi)

is
[
10
, Lemma 3.1]
, but its proof is a slight modification of
the proof of
[
18
, Theorem 1.4]
summarized below and applies in general.
For
g\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
and
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, we denote by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}

the Deligne-Mumford moduli space of stable closed, possibly nodal and disconnected,
Riemann surfaces
(\Sigma,\mathfrak{j})
of arithmetic genus
g

with
\ell
marked points and by

\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}

the real line bundle formed by the determinants of the standard
\bar{\partial}
-operators
on Riemann surfaces.

As with Proposition
3.12
, it is sufficient to assume that
the domain
\Sigma
of
[\mathbf{u}]
is smooth and is stable with its markings.
In both cases, the proof then starts with the isomorphism (
3.29
).
By
[
18
, Lemma 3.2]
, the isomorphism

\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(T(\mathbb{R}\mathcal{M}_{2g-1,\ell}^{\bullet})\!\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}}\big)\!\Big|_{\iota_{g;S^{+},S^{-}}([\mathbf{u}])}\longrightarrow\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(T\mathcal{M}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{C}}\big)\!\Big|_{\mathbf{u}}

obtained by restricting to the first copy of
\Sigma
similarly to (
3.12
)
respects the natural orientation of the domain provided by
[
17
, Proposition 5.9]

and the complex orientation on the target if and only if

(g\!-\!1)\!+\!|S^{-}|
is even.
The summand
(g\!-\!1)
arises from Step
(

⁢
M
2

)
because
the two natural complex orientations of the real dual of
a finite-dimensional complex vector space are the same if and only if
its complex dimension is even; see the beginning of
[
18
, Section 3.1]
.
The summand
|S^{-}|
arises from Steps
(

⁢
M
3

)
and
(

⁢
M
2

)

because the complex vector space in
(

⁢
M
3

)
is the direct sum of the cotangent spaces
at the marked points
z_{i}^{+}
(and it is dualized in Step
(

⁢
M
2

)
).
The analogous comparison of the orientation on the first factor on
the right-hand side of (
3.29
)
induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
and the orientation obtained by restricting to
the first copy of
\Sigma
is provided by Corollary
3.7
.
∎

3.4
Orientations of nodal strata

Let
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
,
g,\ell
, and
B
be as in Theorem
1.2
.
We denote by
\mathcal{N}_{X}X^{\phi}
the normal bundle of
X^{\phi}
in
X
.
The symplectic orientation on
X
and an orientation on
X^{\phi}
,
such as one induced by a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
on
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
determine an orientation on
\mathcal{N}_{X}X^{\phi}
via the short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow TX^{\phi}\longrightarrow TX|_{X^{\phi}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}X^{\phi}\longrightarrow 0

of real vector bundles over
X^{\phi}
.
For each
i\!\in\![\ell]
, let

\textnormal{ev}_{i}\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow X\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i}^{-}\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

be the natural evaluation map at the marked point
z_{i}^{+}

and the universal tangent line orbi-bundles at the marked points
z_{i}^{+}
and
z_{i}^{-}
,
respectively.
The involutions
\sigma
on the domains of the elements of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

induce an
\mathbb{C}
-antilinear isomorphism

\Phi_{i}\!:\mathcal{L}_{i}\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}_{i}^{-}

covering the identity of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
.
Let

\big(\mathcal{L}_{i}\!\otimes\!\mathcal{L}_{i}^{-}\big)^{\mathbb{R}}=\big\{rv\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\Phi_{i}(v)\!:v\!\in\!\mathcal{L}_{i},\,r\!\in\!\mathbb{R}\big\}.

This real line bundle over
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
is canonically oriented
by the standard orientation of
\mathbb{R}
.

Define

\begin{split}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)&=\big\{[\mathbf{u}]\!\in\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!:\,\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}([\mathbf{u}])\!=\!\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+2}([\mathbf{u}])\!\big\},\\
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)&=\big\{[\mathbf{u}]\!\in\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!:\,\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}([\mathbf{u}])\!\in\!X^{\phi}\!\big\}.\end{split}

The short exact sequences

\begin{split}0&\longrightarrow T\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow T\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big|_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)}\longrightarrow\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}^{\,*}TX\longrightarrow 0,\\
0&\longrightarrow T\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow T\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big|_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)}\longrightarrow\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}^{\,*}\mathcal{N}_{X}X^{\phi}\longrightarrow 0\end{split}

induce isomorphisms

\begin{split}\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big)\!\big)\!\big|_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)}&\approx\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big)\!\big)\!\otimes\!\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}^{*}\big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}(TX)\!\big),\\
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big)\!\big)\!\big|_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)}&\approx\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big)\!\big)\!\otimes\!\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}^{*}\big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}(\mathcal{N}_{X}X^{\phi})\!\big)\end{split}

(3.35)

of real line bundles over
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

and
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
, respectively.

We denote by

\iota_{g,\ell}^{\mathbb{C}}\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+2}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\iota_{g,\ell}^{E}\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

(3.36)

the immersion obtained by identifying the marked points
z_{\ell+1}^{+}
and
z_{\ell+1}^{-}

of the domain of each map with
z_{\ell+2}^{+}
and
z_{\ell+2}^{-}
, respectively,
to form a conjugate pair of nodes
and the immersion obtained by identifying the marked point
z_{\ell+1}^{+}

of the domain of each map with
z_{\ell+1}^{-}
to form an
E
-node.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.11
,
it is sufficient to establish the claim over a single element
[\widehat{\mathcal{C}}]

of each connected component of
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\bullet}

parametrizing either connected marked curves or basic marked doublets as in (
3.10
)
with
\Sigma_{1}
connected.
By adding more marked points if necessary, we can choose
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
so that
the irreducible component
\Sigma_{1}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
carrying the marked point
z_{\ell+1}^{+}
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}

and also carries the marked point
z_{1}^{+}
and either

(1)

the marked point
z_{2}^{-}
and no other marked or nodal points

(2)

one non-real node and no other marked or real points.

In the first case,
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
is a genus 0 doublet with 3 conjugate pairs of marked points,
and so
g\!=\!0
,
\ell\!=\!2
, and
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\bullet}
consists of four points
in this case.
Since the degrees of
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mathbb{C}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{C}}}
in (
3.38
)
are even,
the Exact Squares property,
[
18
, Proposition 4.18]
, and
Lemma
3.11
reduce the second case above to the first.

We now assume that
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
is as in the first case above.
Thus,
\iota_{E}([\widehat{\mathcal{C}}])
corresponds to
0\!\in\!\mathbb{R}\!\subset\!\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^{1}

under the diffeomorphism (
3.44
).
The kernel of the surjective operator
\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}
at
\iota_{E}([\widehat{\mathcal{C}}])

consists of constant
\mathbb{R}
-valued functions and thus has a canonical orientation
\mathfrak{o}_{\bar{\partial}}
.
By the assumption in the statement of the lemma,
the orientation of
T_{\iota_{E}([\widehat{\mathcal{C}}])}(\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2})

corresponding to
\mathfrak{o}_{\bar{\partial}}
via the orientation of the real line orbi-bundle (
3.28
)
is given by the diffeomorphism (
3.44
).
Since the
E
-node of
\iota_{E}([\widehat{\mathcal{C}}])
is deformed to a real circle
along the positive direction of
(\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\!\otimes\!\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}^{-})^{\mathbb{R}}
,
the canonical orientation of
(\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\!\otimes\!\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}^{-})^{\mathbb{R}}

at
[\widehat{\mathcal{C}}]
corresponds to the orientation of
T_{\iota_{E}([\widehat{\mathcal{C}}])}(\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\bullet})
.
Thus, the pullback of the orientation
\mathfrak{o}_{\bar{\partial}}\!\otimes\!\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}

by the bottom and left isomorphisms in (
3.39
)
corresponds to the orientation of
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
as a positive point.

The kernel of the surjective operator
\bar{\partial}_{(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{\sigma})}
at
[\widehat{\mathcal{C}}]

consists of
\mathbb{C}
-valued functions that are constant on each of the two components of
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}

and take conjugate values on them.
The orientation
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{\bar{\partial}}
on
\bar{\partial}_{(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{\sigma})}
induced from
the complex orientation on
\mathbb{C}
by restricting these functions on
\Sigma_{1}

corresponds to the orientation
\mathfrak{o}_{\bar{\partial}}\!\otimes\!\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}

via the right isomorphism in (
3.39
).
By
[
18
, Lemma 3.2]
, the top isomorphism in (
3.39
)
identifies
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{\bar{\partial}}
with the orientation of
T_{[\widehat{\mathcal{C}}]}(\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{-1,3}^{\bullet})

obtained from the complex orientation of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}
by restricting to the component
\Sigma_{1}
.
Thus, the pullback of
\mathfrak{o}_{\bar{\partial}}\!\otimes\!\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}

by the right and top isomorphisms in (
3.39
)
also corresponds to the orientation of
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
as a positive point.
This establishes the claim.
∎

Proposition 3.16
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
n
,
J
,
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
g
,
\ell
, and
B
are as in Theorem
1.2
.
The intrinsic and pullback orientations on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.20
) are opposite.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
,
the intrinsic and pullback orientations on

\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
and
\widetilde{\phi}
via (
3.22
) are the same
if and only if
g\!+\!\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\rangle/2\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to establish the claims over an element
[\widehat{\mathbf{u}}]

of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
with smooth domain
(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{\sigma},\widehat{\mathfrak{j}})
.
Let
[\mathbf{u}]\!=\!\iota_{g,\ell}^{E}([\widehat{\mathbf{u}}])
;
the domain
(\Sigma,\sigma,\mathfrak{j})
of
\mathbf{u}
then has one
E
-node and no other nodes.
Let
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
and
\mathcal{C}
be the marked domains of
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}
and
\mathbf{u}
,
respectively,
\widehat{u},u
be their map components, and

\widehat{\mathfrak{M}}=T_{[\widehat{\mathbf{u}}]}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J),\quad\widehat{\mathfrak{M}}^{\prime\prime}=T_{[\widehat{\mathbf{u}}]}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-1,\ell+1}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J),\quad\mathfrak{M}=T_{[\mathbf{u}]}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\prime\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\,.

We denote the relevant tangent spaces of
[\widehat{\mathcal{C}}],[\mathcal{C}]
by
\widehat{\mathcal{M}},\mathcal{M}
, respectively,
the real CR-operators
D_{\widehat{u}^{*}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)},D_{u^{*}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}
by
\widehat{D},D
,
and the homomorphisms from the vector spaces
\mathcal{N}_{X}X^{\phi}|_{\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+1}(\widehat{\mathbf{u}})}

and
(\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\!\otimes\!\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}^{-})^{\mathbb{R}}|_{\widehat{\mathbf{u}}}
to
\{0\}

by
\mathbf{0}_{X^{\phi}}
and
\mathbf{0}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
Let
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}
,
\mathfrak{o}_{X^{\phi}}
, and
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{L}}
be
the orientation on
\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}
induced by the complex orientation of
\mathbb{C}
and
the standard orientation of
\mathbb{R}\!\subset\!\mathbb{C}
,
the orientation on
\det\mathbf{0}_{X^{\phi}}

determined by the real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
on
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
and the orientation on
\det\mathbf{0}_{\mathcal{L}}
determined by the canonical
orientation on
(\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\!\otimes\!\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}^{-})^{\mathbb{R}}
, respectively.
For
k\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, the standard orientation
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}^{k}/\mathbb{R}^{k}}
on
\mathbb{C}^{k}/\mathbb{R}^{k}

agrees with
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}^{\otimes k}
if and only if
(k\!-\!1)/2
is even.

An orientation
\mathfrak{o}
on
\det\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}

determines an orientation
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}
on
\det\bar{\partial}_{(\widehat{\Sigma},\widehat{\sigma})}

), and an orientation

) from

.

similarly determines an orientation

on
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}

and an orientation

on

.

on
\widehat{\mathfrak{M}}
(resp.

on
\mathfrak{M}
)

)

and

(resp.

and

)
via the isomorphism (

), which is associated with the middle row in

.
The intrinsic (resp. pullback) orientation

)

) (resp. (

)),

exact square of Figure

.

Figure 3:
Exact squares of Fredholm operators inducing the orientations of Proposition
3.16

Since
n\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, the Exact Squares property implies that
the right vertical isomorphism in (
3.27
) with
k\!=\!n

respects the orientations
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}^{\otimes n}
,
\mathfrak{o}^{\otimes n}
,
and
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}^{\otimes n}
if and only if
(n\!-\!1)/2
is even.
Thus, the right vertical isomorphism in (
3.27
) with
k\!=\!n

respects the orientations
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}^{\otimes n}
,
\mathfrak{o}^{\otimes n}
, and
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{C}^{n}/\mathbb{R}^{n}}
.
Along with the first statement of Corollary
3.9
,
this implies that the left vertical isomorphism in (
3.27
),
which is associated with the left column in the first exact square of Figure
3
,
respects the orientations
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{D}
,
\mathfrak{o}_{D}
, and
\mathfrak{o}_{X^{\phi}}
if and only if

g\!-\!1\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
Since the dimension of
X^{\phi}
is odd and the dimension of
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}
is of the same parity
as
g
,
the Exact Squares property in turn implies that the top row in this square
does not respect the orientations
\mathfrak{o}_{D}
,
\mathfrak{o}_{in}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{\mathcal{M}}
.

By Lemma
3.15
,
the left vertical isomorphism in (
3.39
),
which is associated with the right column in the second exact square of Figure
3
,
respects the orientations
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{\mathcal{M}}
,
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{M}}
, and
\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{L}}
.
The Exact Squares property then implies that the top row in this square
also respects the orientations
\mathfrak{o}_{D}
,
\mathfrak{o}_{pu}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\widehat{\mathfrak{o}}_{\mathcal{M}}
.
Comparing the conclusions concerning the top rows in the two diagrams,
we obtain the first claim of the proposition.
The second claim is obtained in the same way using the second statement of
Corollary
3.9
instead of the first.
∎

With
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
,
g
,
\ell
, and
B
as in Theorem
1.2

and
i\!\in\![\ell]
, let

D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

be the subspace of maps from domains
\Sigma
so that one of the irreducible components
\Sigma_{i}

of
\Sigma

is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
which has precisely one node, carries only the marked points
z_{i}^{+}

and
z_{\ell+1}^{\pm}
, and is contracted by the map.
The (virtual) normal bundle
\mathcal{N}D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}
of
D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}
in

\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

is canonically isomorphic to the complex line bundle of the smoothings of
the above node of
\Sigma_{i}
, as in
[
16
, Lemma 5.2]
.
The complex orientation on
\mathcal{N}D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}

and an orientation on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
determine an orientation
on
D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}
.

Corollary 3.17
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
,
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
g
,
\ell
, and
B
are as in Theorem
1.2

and
i\!\in\![\ell]
.
The sign of the diffeomorphism

D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}\approx\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

(3.40)

induced by dropping the last conjugate pair of marked points is
\pm 1

with respect to the orientations on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(X,B)

and
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell+1}^{\phi;\bullet}(X,B)

induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.20
)
and the complex orientation on
\mathcal{N}D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
,
the same property holds with (
3.20
) replaced by (
3.22
).

Proof.

We denote by
\mathfrak{D}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,3}^{\phi;\pm}(X)\!\subset\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{-1,3}^{\phi;\bullet}(0;J)

the topological component of maps from domains consisting of two copies of
\mathbb{P}^{1}

one of which carries the marked points
z_{1}^{+},z_{2}^{\pm},z_{3}^{+}
.
Let

\rho\!:\mathfrak{D}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,3}^{\phi;\pm}(X)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,3}(0;J)\!\approx\!X

(3.41)

be the diffeomorphism obtained by restricting each map to the copy of
\mathbb{P}^{1}

carrying the marked points
z_{1}^{+},z_{2}^{\pm},z_{3}^{+}
and

\varphi_{i}^{\pm}\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,3}^{\phi;\pm}(X)

be the map so that
\textnormal{ev}_{i}\!=\!\textnormal{ev}_{3}\!\circ\!\varphi_{i}^{\pm}
.

We denote by
\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}\!\subset\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+3}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

the image of the open embedding

\widetilde{\iota}_{g;i}^{\pm}\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\times\!\mathfrak{D}\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,3}^{\phi;\pm}(X)\longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+3}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)

(3.42)

sending a pair of maps to their disjoint union and re-indexing the conjugate pairs
of marked points on the two domains as

(1,\ldots,i\!-\!1,i,i\!+\!1,\ldots,\ell)\longrightarrow\big(1,\ldots,i\!-\!1,\ell\!+\!2,i\!+\!1,\ldots,\ell\big),\quad(1,2,3)\longrightarrow(i,\ell\!+\!1,\ell\!+\!3).

Define

\begin{split}\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\prime\pm;\bullet}=\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}\!\cap\!\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+3}^{\prime\phi;\bullet}(B;J)&\equiv\big\{[\mathbf{u}]\!\in\!\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}\!:\!\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+2}([\mathbf{u}])\!=\!\textnormal{ev}_{\ell+3}([\mathbf{u}])\!\big\}\\
&=\big\{\widetilde{\iota}_{g;i}^{\pm}\!\circ\!(\textnormal{id},\varphi_{i}^{\pm})\!\big\}\big(\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\!\big).\end{split}

An orientation on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g-2,\ell+3}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
induces an orientation
on
\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\prime\pm;\bullet}
via the first isomorphism in (
3.35
).

The first node-identifying immersion in (
3.36
) restricts to a diffeomorphism

q\!:\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\prime\pm;\bullet}\longrightarrow D_{i}^{\pm;\bullet}\,.

(3.43)

The composition
q\!\circ\!\widetilde{\iota}_{g;i}^{\pm}\!\circ\!(\textnormal{id},\varphi_{i}^{\pm})

is the inverse of (
3.40
).
By Proposition
3.12
with
g_{2}\!=\!-1
and
B_{2}\!=\!0
,
the diffeomorphism (
3.42
) is orientation-preserving with respect
to the orientations induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via
either (
3.20
) or (
3.22
),
if
L
admits a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
lifting
\phi
in the last case.
By the first claim of Proposition
3.13
with
B\!=\!0
,
the sign of the diffeomorphism (
3.41
) is
\pm 1
with respect to
the orientation on the domain induced via (
3.20
) and
the complex orientation on the target.
Thus, the sign of the diffeomorphism

\widetilde{\iota}_{g;i}^{\pm}\!\circ\!(\textnormal{id},\varphi_{i}^{\pm})\!:\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)\longrightarrow\widetilde{D}_{i}^{\prime\pm;\bullet}

is
\pm 1
as well.
By the first claim of Proposition
3.14
,
the diffeomorphism (
3.43
) is orientation-preserving with respect to the orientations
induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.20
)
as described above.
This establishes the first claim.

If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi

and the moduli spaces of real maps are oriented via (
3.22
),
then the sign of the diffeomorphism (
3.41
) is
\mp 1

by the second claim of Proposition
3.13
with
g\!=\!0

and the diffeomorphism (
3.43
) is orientation-reversing
by the second claim of Proposition
3.14
.
Thus, the sign of (
3.40
) is
\pm 1
in this case as well.
∎

3.5
Spaces of rational maps

Let
S^{1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the unit circle in
\mathbb{C}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{1}
;
it is preserved by the anti-holomorphic involutions
\tau
and
\eta
defined
in (
2.1
).
For
c\!=\!\tau,\eta
, we orient the group
G_{c}
of holomorphic automorphisms
of
(\mathbb{P}^{1},c)
via the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow T_{\textnormal{id}}S^{1}\longrightarrow T_{\textnormal{id}}G_{c}\longrightarrow T_{0}\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow 0

from the standard orientations of
S^{1}
and
\mathbb{C}
.
For
\ell\!\geq\!2
, we denote by
\mathcal{M}_{0,\ell}^{\tau}
the uncompactified moduli space of
equivalence classes of
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)
with
\ell
pairs of conjugate marked points.
The Deligne-Mumford compactification
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
of
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau}

includes 3 additional stable real two-component nodal curves.
A diffeomorphism of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
with a closed interval is given by

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}\longrightarrow[0,\infty],\quad\big[(z_{1}^{+},z_{1}^{-}),(z_{2}^{+},z_{2}^{-})\big]\longrightarrow\frac{z_{2}^{+}\!-\!z_{1}^{-}}{z_{2}^{-}\!-\!z_{1}^{-}}:\frac{z_{2}^{+}\!-\!z_{1}^{+}}{z_{2}^{-}\!-\!z_{1}^{+}}=\frac{|1\!-\!z_{1}^{+}/z_{2}^{-}|^{2}}{|z_{1}^{+}\!-\!z_{2}^{+}|^{2}}\,.

(3.44)

It takes the two-component curve with
z_{1}^{+}
and
z_{2}^{-}
on the same component to
0

and the two-component curve with
z_{1}^{+}
and
z_{2}^{+}
on the same component to
\infty
.

For a real symplectic manifold
(X,\omega,\phi)
,

J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}^{\phi}
,
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, and
c\!=\!\tau,\eta
, we denote by

\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)\subset\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,\ell}^{\phi}(B;J)

the subspace of equivalence classes of maps from
(\mathbb{P}^{1},c)
and by

\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)
the space of real degree
B

J
-holomorphic
(parametrized) maps from
(\mathbb{P}^{1},c)
to
(X,\phi)

with
\ell
conjugate pairs of (distinct) marked points.
The group
G_{c}
acts on this space by

\displaystyle G_{c}\!\times\!\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J),

\displaystyle g\!\cdot\!\big(u,(z_{1}^{+},z_{1}^{-}),\ldots,(z_{\ell}^{+},z_{\ell}^{-})\!\big)=\big(u\!\circ\!g^{-1},(g(z_{1}^{+}),g(z_{1}^{-})\!),\ldots,(g(z_{\ell}^{+}),g(z_{\ell}^{-})\!)\!\big).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)\big/G_{c}\,,

(3.45)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T_{\mathbf{u}}\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)\!\big)

\displaystyle\approx\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T_{\textnormal{id}}G_{c}\big)\!\otimes\!\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T_{[\mathbf{u}]}\mathfrak{M}_{0,0}^{\phi,c}(B;J)\big).

(3.46)

Since
G_{\tau}
has two topological components, an orientation on
\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)

might not descend to the quotient (
3.45
) via (
3.46
).

The (virtual) tangent space of
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\phi,c}(B;J)
at a
(\phi,c)
-real map
u

is the index (as a K-theory class) of the linearization of the
\bar{\partial}_{J}
-operator at
u
.
A trivialization of the real bundle pair
u^{*}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)
over
(S^{1},c|_{S^{1}})

determines an orientation on this index, or equivalently on
\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}|_{u}
,
via a pinching construction as in the proofs of
[
8
, Proposition 8.1.4]

and
[
6
, Lemma 2.5]
.
If
c\!=\!\tau
, a continuously varying collection of such trivializations
is determined by a relative spin structure on the real vector bundle
TX^{\phi}

over
X^{\phi}\!\subset\!\!X
;
see the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 8.1.1]
or
[
23
, Theorem 6.36]
.

An orientation on
\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\phi,c}(B;J)
induces orientations on
the spaces
\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)

with
\ell\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
by assigning the fibers of the forgetful morphism

\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,c}(B;J)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{P}_{\ell-1}^{\phi,c}(B;J)

(3.47)

the canonical complex orientation of the tangent space at
the first marked point in the last conjugate pair of marked points of each marked map.
By the proof of
[
9
, Theorem 6.6]
with (
3.22
)
replaced by (
3.20
),
the relative spin structure on
TX^{\phi}
associated to
a real orientation on
(X,\omega,\phi)
induces an orientation on
\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)

that descends to this quotient and extends to the stable map compactification.
If
\ell\!\geq\!2
, we can take
(X,B)\!=\!(\textnormal{pt},0)
in (
3.45
) and (
3.46
)
and obtain an orientation on

\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau}=\mathfrak{M}_{0,2}^{\textnormal{id},\tau}(\textnormal{pt},0)\,.

(3.48)

This orientation agrees with the orientation on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
determined by
the diffeomorphism (
3.44
).

The construction of the orientation on the real line orbi-bundle (
3.28
)
in the proof of
[
17
, Proposition 5.9]
involves
a somewhat arbitrary sign choice for the Serre duality isomorphism
in Step
(

⁢
M
2

)
on page
(

⁢
M
2

)
of the present paper.
The (real) dimensions of its domain and target are
3(g\!-\!1)\!+\!2\ell
.
If
g\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, this choice thus has no effect on the homotopy class of this isomorphism
or the resulting orientation of the real line bundle (
3.28
).
If
g\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, changing this choice changes the resulting orientation of (
3.28
)
and the orientation on the moduli space
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
of real maps
determined by a real orientation via (
3.29
).
In light of
[
18
, Proposition 4.18]
, the above sign choice is determined
by a choice of orientation
of the real line bundle (
3.28
) over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
.
Since the real CR-operator
\bar{\partial}_{(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)}
is surjective and its kernel consists
of the constant
\mathbb{R}
-valued functions on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
an orientation on (
3.28
) over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}

is determined by an orientation on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
.
We orient
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
by the diffeomorphism (
3.44
).

Proposition 3.18
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
,
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
,
\ell
, and
B
are as in Theorem
1.2
.
The orientations on
\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)
induced
by the associated relative spin structure on the real vector bundle
TX^{\phi}

over
X^{\phi}\!\subset\!X
and by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.29
) and (
3.20
)
are the same if and only if
\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\rangle\!\not\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
,
the former orientation and the orientation induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.29
)
and (
3.22
) with
\widetilde{\sigma}\!=\!\widetilde{\tau}

are the same if and only if
\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\rangle\!\cong\!2,4
​​​
\mod 8
.
If
L^{\widetilde{\phi}}\!\longrightarrow\!X^{\phi}
is orientable, then
the orientations on
\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)
induced by
the associated spin structure on
TX^{\phi}
and the latter orientation are opposite.

Proof.

Since the fibers of the forgetful morphism (
3.47
) are canonically oriented,
it is sufficient to establish the claims for
\ell\!=\!2
.
Orienting
\mathfrak{M}_{0,2}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)
via (
3.46
) with
\ell\!=\!2

and (
3.47
) with
\ell\!=\!1,2
from an orientation on
\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}|_{u}

and the complex orientation of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is equivalent to orienting this moduli space
via the isomorphism

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\mathfrak{M}_{0,2}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)\!\big)\approx\mathfrak{f}^{*}\!\big(\Lambda^{\textnormal{top}}_{\mathbb{R}}(T\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau})\!\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}\big)

from the chosen orientation of
\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}|_{u}
and
the orientation of
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau}
determined by (
3.45
) and (
3.46
)
with
(X,B)\!=\!(\textnormal{pt},0)
.
Since the dimension of
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau}
and the index of
D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}
are odd,
the orientation on
\mathfrak{M}_{0,2}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)

induced in this way is the opposite of the orientation induced
via the canonical isomorphism (
3.29
) with
(g,\ell)\!=\!(0,2)

from the same orientations on
\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}|_{u}
and
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau}
.
By the sentence below (
3.48
) and the following paragraph,
the orientation on
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{\tau}
used in all of the relevant approaches
to orienting
\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)
is the same.
The relevant orientations on
\det D_{(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)}

are compared by Corollary
3.10
with
(V,\varphi)
replaced
by
u^{*}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)
.
Taking into account that

\big\langle c_{1}(TX,\omega),B\big\rangle=2\big\langle c_{1}(L),B\big\rangle

if
\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)\!\neq\!\emptyset
, we obtain the claims.
∎

Corollary 3.19
.

Suppose
(X,\omega,\phi)
,
J
,
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
, and
\ell
are as in Theorem
1.2
.
The natural isomorphism

\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{0,\ell}^{\phi}(0;J)\approx\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\ell}\!\times\!X^{\phi}

(3.49)

is orientation-reversing with respect to the orientation on
the left-hand side induced by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
via (
3.29
) and (
3.20
),
the orientation on
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\ell}
induced via the diffeomorphism (
3.44
),
and the orientation on
X^{\phi}
determined by
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
.
If in addition
\widetilde{\phi}
is a conjugation on
L
lifting
\phi
,
the same property holds with (
3.20
) replaced by (
3.22
).

Remark 3.20
.

The orientation on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}^{\tau}
determined by the diffeomorphism (
3.44
)
is as above
[
9
, Lemma 5.4]
and in
[
5
, Section 12.3]
.
It is the opposite of the orientation taken
in
[
16
, Section 3]
and
[

, Section 1.4]
,
which is also used in
[

]
and
[
25
]
.
The order of the two factors on the right-hand side of (
3.46
)
is opposite from
[
18
, (1.14)]
.
This does not impact the orientation on
\mathfrak{M}_{0,\ell}^{\phi,\tau}(B;J)

induced from an orientation of
\mathfrak{P}_{\ell}^{\phi}(B;J)

whenever

and

are as in Theorem

because the dimension (

) of

is then even.
However, this reverses the induced orientation on the moduli spaces
with

.
Thus, the property stated after (

) with the present conventions
is equivalent to this property with the conventions in
[

]
.

Remark 3.21
.

Let

,

with

and

replaced by

.
The terms
\det(2\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)})
in (

) and (

)
and

in (

)
could be oriented either via the canonical orientation (as was done for the purposes

-

,

,
and

and

)
or via the projection orientation.
The resulting orientations on

would differ
by
(-1)^{\varepsilon}
with

this follows from Lemma
3.1
with

.
This would make the isomorphism (

) orientation-preserving in the first claim
of Proposition
3.12
,
add
g\!-\!1
to the parity conditions in Propositions

and
3.16
, and switch the conditions in the two claims of

3.13
, but would not impact on

3.17
or

.
The Step
(

⁢
M
4

)
on page
(

⁢
M
4

)
is
carried out in

]
via (

) with

and the canonical orientations on

and

.
Using the projection orientations on these factors instead would change the induced
orientations on the line bundle (
3.28
) and
on the moduli space
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(B;J)
by

.
Since this sign is reversed by changing the preferred choice of the orientation

, as discussed above, this change is immaterial.

4
Updates on past work and corrections

4.1
Real-orientable symplectic manifolds,
[
19
, Sections 1.1,2.1,2.2]

Sufficient conditions for a real symplectic manifold
(X,\omega,\phi)
to admit
a real orientation in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 1.2]
,
i.e. a triple
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
as in Definition
1.1
in the present paper
with
(V,\varphi)\!=\!(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)
and a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
on
L
lifting
\phi
,
are provided in
[
19
, Section 1.1]
.
As indicated below, some real symplectic manifolds admit real orientations in
the weaker sense of Definition
1.1
without a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
on
L
.
The next proposition is a refined analogue of
[
19
, Proposition 1.2]

for the real orientations of Definition
1.1
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
(X,\omega,\phi)
be a connected real symplectic manifold such that

H_{1}(X;\mathbb{Q})=0\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad H^{2}(X^{\phi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})=0.

The collection of the real orientations
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})

on
(X,\omega,\phi)
can be identified with

\big\{\mu\!\in\!H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})\!:c_{1}(X,\omega)\!=\!\mu\!-\!\phi^{*}\mu\big\}\!\times\!H^{1}(X^{\phi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\!\times\!\mathbb{Z}_{2}\subset H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})\!\times\!H^{1}(X^{\phi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\!\times\!\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

Proof.

The complex line bundles
L\!\longrightarrow\!X
such that

\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}(TX)\approx L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L}

(4.1)

correspond via
c_{1}
to the elements
\mu\!\in\!H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
such that

c_{1}(X,\omega)\!=\!\mu\!-\!\phi^{*}\mu
.
Since

w_{2}(TX^{\phi}\!\oplus\!L^{*}|_{X^{\phi}})=0\in H^{2}(X^{\phi};\mathbb{Z}_{2}),

the set of spin structures on
TX^{\phi}\!\oplus\!L^{*}|_{X^{\phi}}

for each complex line bundle
L\!\longrightarrow\!X
can be identified with
H^{1}(X^{\phi};\mathbb{Z}_{2})
;
see the SpinPin 1 and 2 properties in
[
5
, Section 1.2]
.
By
[
6
, Lemma 2.7]
,
H_{1}(X;\mathbb{Q})\!=\!0
and (
4.1
)
imply that the real line bundle pairs
\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}(TX,\textnormal{d}\phi)
and

(L\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\phi^{*}\overline{L},\phi_{L}^{\otimes})
are isomorphic.

It remains to show that a real line bundle pair
(L,\widetilde{\phi})
over
(X,\phi)

has precisely two homotopy classes of automorphisms if
H_{1}(X;\mathbb{Q})\!=\!0
.
Any such homotopy class contains an automorphism

(L,\widetilde{\phi})\longrightarrow(L,\widetilde{\phi})\qquad v\longrightarrow\rho(x)v~~\forall\,v\!\in\!L_{x},~x\!\in\!X,

(4.2)

for some continuous map
\rho\!:X\!\longrightarrow\!S^{1}
such that
\rho(x)\rho(\phi(x)\!)\!=\!1

for all
x\!\in\!X
.
Since
H_{1}(X;\mathbb{Q})\!=\!0
, the homomorphism
\rho_{*}\!:\pi_{1}(X)\!\longrightarrow\!\pi_{1}(S^{1})
is trivial.
Thus,
\rho
lifts over the covering map

\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow S^{1},\qquad\theta\longrightarrow\textnormal{e}^{2\pi\mathfrak{i}\theta},

to a continuous map
\widetilde{\rho}\!:X\!\longrightarrow\!S^{1}
such that

\widetilde{\rho}(x)\!+\!\widetilde{\rho}(\phi(x)\!)\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
for all
x\!\in\!X
.
By adding an integer to
\widetilde{\rho}
, we can assume that

\widetilde{\rho}(x)\!+\!\widetilde{\rho}\big(\phi(x)\!\big)=\epsilon~~\forall\,x\!\in\!X

for some
\epsilon\!\in\!\{0,1\}
.
The family of automorphisms

(L,\widetilde{\phi})\longrightarrow(L,\widetilde{\phi}),\quad\tau\!\times\!v\longrightarrow\begin{cases}\textnormal{e}^{2\pi\mathfrak{i}\tau\widetilde{\rho}(x)}v,&\hbox{if}~\epsilon\!=\!0;\\
-\textnormal{e}^{\pi\mathfrak{i}\tau(2\widetilde{\rho}(x)-1)}v,&\hbox{if}~\epsilon\!=\!1;\end{cases}\qquad\forall\,v\!\in\!L_{x},~x\!\in\!X,

is then a homotopy from
\pm\textnormal{id}_{L}
to the isomorphism (
4.2
).

On the other hand, the automorphisms
\textnormal{id}_{L}
and
-\textnormal{id}_{L}
of
(L,\widetilde{\phi})

are not homotopic.
This is immediate if
X^{\phi}\!\neq\!\emptyset
because

\rho(x)\!=\!\pm 1
for every
x\!\in\!X^{\phi}
.
If
X^{\phi}\!=\!\emptyset
, we can choose a continuous map

\alpha\!:S^{1}\longrightarrow X\qquad\hbox{s.t.}\quad\phi\!\circ\!\alpha=\alpha\!\circ\!\mathfrak{a},

where
\mathfrak{a}\!:S^{1}\!\longrightarrow\!S^{1}
is the antipodal involution.
By the proof of
[
6
, Lemma 2.4]
, the automorphisms
\textnormal{id}_{\alpha^{*}L}
and
-\textnormal{id}_{\alpha^{*}L}

of the real bundle pair
\alpha^{*}(L,\widetilde{\phi})
over
(S^{1},\mathfrak{a})
are not homotopic.
∎

If
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,
\mathbf{a}\!\equiv\!(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})\!\in\!(\mathbb{Z}^{+})^{k}
,
and
X_{n;\mathbf{a}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
(resp.
X_{2m;\mathbf{a}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}
)
is a complete intersection of multi-degree
\mathbf{a}
preserved by
\tau_{n}
(resp.
\eta_{2m}
),
then
\tau_{n;\mathbf{a}}\!\equiv\!\tau_{n}|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}}}
(resp.
\eta_{2m;\mathbf{a}}\!\equiv\!\eta_{2m}|_{X_{2m;\mathbf{a}}}
)
is an anti-symplectic involution on
X_{n;\mathbf{a}}
(resp.
X_{2m;\mathbf{a}}
)
with respect to the symplectic form
\omega_{n;\mathbf{a}}\!=\!\omega_{n}|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}}}

(resp.
\omega_{2m;\mathbf{a}}\!=\!\omega_{2m}|_{X_{2m;\mathbf{a}}}
).
Sufficient conditions on
(n,\mathbf{a})
and
(m,\mathbf{a})

for
(X_{n;\mathbf{a}},\omega_{n;\mathbf{a}},\tau_{n;\mathbf{a}})
and
(X_{2m;\mathbf{a}},\omega_{2m;\mathbf{a}},\eta_{2m;\mathbf{a}})

to admit a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
with a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
on
L
lifting

\phi\!=\!\tau_{n;\mathbf{a}},\eta_{2m;\mathbf{a}}

are provided by
[
19
, Proposition 1.4]
.
If these conditions are satisfied, the real symplectic manifold under consideration
carries a natural real orientation in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 1.2]

with

L=\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{n}}\big((n\!-\!a_{1}\!-\!\ldots\!-\!a_{k})/2\big)\!\big)\big|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}}}\longrightarrow X_{n;\mathbf{a}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{n-1},

where
n\!\equiv\!2m
in the case of
(X_{2m;\mathbf{a}},\omega_{2m;\mathbf{a}},\eta_{2m;\mathbf{a}})
.

Dropping the requirement of the existence of a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
on
L
lifting
\eta_{2m;\mathbf{a}}

immediately leads to weakening the condition

a_{1}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!a_{k}\equiv 2m\mod 4

(4.3)

in
[
19
, Proposition 1.4(2)]
to the mod 2 equivalence
(a complex line bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}
needs to be of an even degree to admit
a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
lifting
\eta_{2m}
).
However, the condition (
4.3
) holds for any
\eta_{2m}
-real complete intersection

X_{2m;\mathbf{a}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}
of multi-degree
\mathbf{a}

because the odd components
a_{i}
of
\mathbf{a}
come in pairs
if such a complete intersection exists.
Thus, every
\eta_{2m}
-real complete intersection
X_{2m;\mathbf{a}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{2m-1}

admits a natural real orientation in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
On the other hand, no difference is made in the case of
(X_{n;\mathbf{a}},\omega_{n;\mathbf{a}},\tau_{n;\mathbf{a}})
,
as every complex line bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
admits a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
lifting
\tau_{n}
.

Suppose
(\Sigma,\sigma)
is a smooth compact connected symmetric surface of genus
g
and

(X,\phi)
is a real bundle pair over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
of degree
d
.
By a standard construction, any precompact neighborhood of
\Sigma\!\subset\!X

admits a symplectic form
\omega
so that
\phi^{*}\omega\!=\!-\omega
and

\omega|_{T\Sigma}
is a volume form on
\Sigma
; see
[
7
, (2.9)]
, for example.
A complex line bundle
L
over
X
satisfying (
4.1
) exists
if and only if
d\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
By
[
1
, Propositions 4.1,4.2]
,
(X,\phi)
admits a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})

as in Definition
1.1
in such a case,
as the first equation in (
1.2
) imposes no restriction on
L
.
For any such real orientation,
L
admits a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
lifting
\phi

if and only if either
\Sigma^{\sigma}\!\neq\!\emptyset
or
g\!+\!d/2\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular, every smooth compact connected genus
g
symmetric surface
(\Sigma,\sigma)

admits a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
as in Definition
1.1
,
but
(\Sigma,\sigma)
does not admit a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
with a conjugation
\widetilde{\sigma}

lifting
\sigma
if (and only if)
\Sigma^{\sigma}\!=\!\emptyset
and
g\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
This is the motivation behind the introduction of the weaker notion of real orientation
in
[
10
]
.

4.2
Associated relative spin structures,
[
18
, Corollary 3.8]

The second and third claims of Corollary
3.10
are special cases
of the base cases of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8]
.
Unfortunately, the derivation of the full statement of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8]

from its base cases is based on the implicit assumption that the isomorphisms as
in (
3.21
) induced in a manner similar to (
3.22
)
commute with disjoint unions as in (
3.23
).
This is indeed the case in the setting relevant to the derivation of the full statement
of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8(2)]
from its base cases in
[
18
]
;
thus, this statement is correct.
However, this commutativity may not hold in the setting relevant to the derivation
of the full statement of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8(1)]
;
this statement needs to be corrected as described below.
Fortunately,
[
18
, Corollary 3.8]
beyond its base cases has not been elsewhere yet
(to the best of our knowledge).

Let
D_{(V,\varphi)}
be a real CR-operator on a rank
n
real bundle pair
(V,\varphi)

over a smooth symmetric surface
(\Sigma,\sigma)
and
(L,\widetilde{\phi})
be a rank 1 real
bundle pair over
(\Sigma,\sigma)
.
We assume that the real vector bundle
V^{\varphi}
over the fixed locus
\Sigma^{\sigma}

of
\sigma
is orientable.
The subject of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8]
is a comparison between two homotopy classes
of isomorphisms

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\det\!\big(n\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big),

(4.4)

determined by trivializations of
(L,\widetilde{\phi})
over cutting circles for
(\Sigma,\sigma)
;
these homotopy classes correspond to orientations of

(\det D_{(V,\varphi)})\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det(n\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)})\!\big)
.

In
[
18
, Corollary 3.8(1)]
, it is assumed that
\Sigma^{\sigma}
separates
\Sigma

and has
m
topological components,
\Sigma^{\sigma}_{1},\ldots,\Sigma^{\sigma}_{m}
.
The symmetric surface
(\Sigma,\sigma)
can then be obtained by doubling a bordered surface
\Sigma^{b}

along its boundary as in
[
13
, (1.6)]
.
The boundary
\partial\Sigma^{b}
of
\Sigma^{b}
must have
m
topological components as well.
For each
i\!=\!1,\ldots,m
, let

\varepsilon_{i}(L)=\begin{cases}0,&\hbox{if}~w_{1}(L^{\widetilde{\phi}})|_{\Sigma^{\sigma}_{i}}\!=\!0,\\
1,&\hbox{if}~w_{1}(L^{\widetilde{\phi}})|_{\Sigma^{\sigma}_{i}}\!\neq\!0.\end{cases}

We define
m_{1}(L)\!\in\![m]
to be the sum of these numbers
\varepsilon_{i}(L)
.
The necessary and sufficient condition for the two resulting homotopy classes of
isomorphism (
4.4
) to be the same in
[
18
, Corollary 3.8(1)]

should be replaced by

\deg L-mm_{1}(L)-\binom{m_{1}(L)}{2}\in 4\mathbb{Z}\,,

(4.5)

for the reasons below.

By the usual pinching construction, as the proof of
[
23
, Lemma 6.37]
,
a trivialization
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
of the real vector bundle

V^{\varphi}\!\oplus\!2L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
over
\Sigma^{\sigma}
and
a choice of a half-surface
\Sigma^{b}
for
(\Sigma,\sigma)

determine a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(V\oplus 2L,\varphi\oplus 2\widetilde{\phi})}\approx\det\!\big(\!(n\!+\!2)\bar{\partial}_{(\Sigma,\sigma)}\big)\,.

(4.6)

Similarly to (
3.22
), this homotopy class determines
a homotopy class of isomorphisms (
4.4
), called
the
stabilization orientation induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
in
[
18
, Section 3.2]
.
Along with the isomorphism
\Phi_{L}
in (
1.7
) composed with the projection of the target
to the first factor,
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
also determines a relative spin structure on
the real vector bundle
V^{\varphi}
over
\Sigma^{\sigma}

and thus another homotopy class of isomorphisms (
4.4
),
called the
associated relative spin
(or
ARS
)
orientation
induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
in
[
18
, Section 3.2]
.
These two homotopy classes are the same if and only if (
4.5
) holds.

The argument in the proof of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8]
starts
by pinching off two circles near each component
\Sigma^{\sigma}_{i}
of
\Sigma
,
one circle on each side of
\Sigma^{\sigma}_{i}
, to form a nodal symmetric surface
(\Sigma_{0},\sigma_{0})
.
The domain of its normalization,

q\!:(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{0},\widetilde{\sigma}_{0})\longrightarrow(\Sigma_{0},\sigma_{0}),

(4.7)

consists of a copy
(\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1},\tau_{i})

of
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\tau)
for each component
\Sigma^{\sigma}_{i}
of
\Sigma
and
a doublet
(\Sigma_{0}^{+}\!\sqcup\!\Sigma_{0}^{-},\sigma^{\prime})
as in (
3.10
).
We choose
\Sigma_{0}^{+}
so that it is contained in the image of
\Sigma^{b}
under
the pinching procedure.
Let
x_{i}^{+}\!\in\!\Sigma_{0}
be the node separating
\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1}
from
\Sigma_{0}^{+}
.
We deform the real bundle pairs
(V,\varphi)
and
(L,\widetilde{\phi})
to
real bundle pairs
(V_{0},\varphi_{0})
and
(L_{0},\widetilde{\phi}_{0})
over
(\Sigma_{0},\sigma_{0})

so that
\deg L_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}^{+}}\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and thus

\deg L-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\deg L_{0}|_{\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1}}\in 4\mathbb{Z}.

(4.8)

Let
(V_{i},\varphi_{i})\!\equiv\!(V_{0},\varphi_{0})|_{(\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1},\tau_{i})}
and

(L_{i},\widetilde{\phi}_{i})\!\equiv\!(L_{0},\widetilde{\phi}_{0})|_{(\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1},\tau_{i})}
.

A real CR-operator
D_{(V_{0},\varphi_{0})}
on
(V_{0},\varphi_{0})
lifts to
a real CR-operator
q^{*}D_{(V_{0},\varphi_{0})}
on
q^{*}(V_{0},\varphi_{0})
as above Lemma
3.4
.
The latter is isomorphic to the direct sum of real CR-operators
D_{(V_{i},\varphi_{i})}

on
(V_{i},\varphi_{i})
and a CR-operator
D_{V_{0}^{+}}
on
V_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}^{+}}
.
There is a canonical homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\det D_{(V_{0},\varphi_{0})}\,.

Similarly to (
2.4
) and (
3.16
), there is also an isomorphism

\big(\!\det D_{(V_{0},\varphi_{0})}\big)\!\otimes\!\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(V_{0}|_{x_{i}^{+}}\big)\approx\big(\!\det D_{V_{0}^{+}}\big)\!\otimes\!\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}\big(\!\det D_{(V_{i},\varphi_{i})}\big)\,.

Combining the last two isomorphisms with the canonical complex orientations on

\Lambda_{\mathbb{R}}^{\textnormal{top}}(V_{0}|_{x_{i}^{+}})
and
\det D_{V_{0}^{+}}
, we obtain a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(V,\varphi)}\approx\big(\!\det D_{(V_{1},\varphi_{1})}\big)\!\otimes\!\ldots\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det D_{(V_{m},\varphi_{m})}\big).

(4.9)

The trivialization
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
determines a relative spin structure on
the real vector bundle
V_{i}^{\varphi_{i}}\!\oplus\!2L_{i}^{\widetilde{\phi}_{i}}
over
the fixed locus
\Sigma^{\sigma}_{i}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{1}_{i}
of
\tau_{i}
for each
i\!\in\![m]
and thus
a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\det D_{(V_{i},\varphi_{i})}\approx\det\!\big(n\bar{\partial}_{(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{i},\tau_{i})}\big).

(4.10)

By the assumption that
\deg L_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}^{+}}\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, the diagram

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
