(4.11)

with the vertical isomorphisms corresponding to
the ARS orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
commutes up to homotopy.

The analogue of the diagram (
4.11
) for the isomorphisms (
4.6
)
induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
also commutes.
Similarly to the reasoning in the proof of Corollary
3.6
,
Lemma
3.2
then implies that the diagram (
4.11
)
with the vertical isomorphisms corresponding to
the stabilization orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
commutes up to homotopy
if and only if the number

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[m]\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\big(1\!+\!(\deg L_{0}|_{\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1}}\!-\!1)(\deg L_{0}|_{\mathbb{P}_{j}^{1}}\!-\!1)\!\big)\cong\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[m]\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\big(\varepsilon_{i}(L)\!+\!\varepsilon_{j}(L)\!+\!\varepsilon_{i}(L)\varepsilon_{j}(L)\!\big)\mod 2

(4.12)

is even.
By Lemma 3.4 and Corollary 3.6 in
[
18
]
, the homotopy classes of
isomorphisms (
4.10
)
corresponding to the ARS and stabilization orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}

are the same if and only
\deg L_{0}|_{\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1}}\!-\!\varepsilon_{i}(L)\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
.
Combining this with the end of the previous paragraph and (
4.8
),
we conclude that the homotopy classes of left vertical isomorphisms in (
4.11
)
corresponding to the ARS and stabilization orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}

are the same if and only if (
4.5
) holds.

In
[
18
, Corollary 3.8(2)]
, the fixed locus
\Sigma^{\sigma}
of
\sigma
is not assumed to be
separating, but the real line bundle
L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
is assumed to be orientable.
There is again a choice of a half-surface
\Sigma^{b}
doubling to
(\Sigma,\sigma)
;
its boundary consists of
\Sigma^{\sigma}
and so-called
cross-caps
,
i.e. circles with fixed-point free involutions.
In addition to a trivialization
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
of
the real vector bundle
V^{\varphi}\!\oplus\!2L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
over
\Sigma^{\sigma}
,
there is also a trivialization
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
of the real bundle pair

(V\!\oplus\!2L,\varphi\!\oplus\!2\widetilde{\phi})
over the union
\partial_{1}^{c}\Sigma^{b}
of the crosscaps.
By the pinching construction in the proof of
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]
,

\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
,
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
, and
\Sigma^{b}
determine a homotopy class
of isomorphisms (
4.6
) and thus a homotopy class of isomorphisms (
4.4
),
called the
stabilization orientation induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}

in
[
18
, Section 3.2]
.
Since the real line bundle
2L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
is orientable,
the trivialization
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
and the canonical homotopy class of trivializations
of
2L^{\widetilde{\phi}}
determine a homotopy class of trivializations
of the real vector bundle
V^{\varphi}
over
\Sigma^{\sigma}
.
The trivialization
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
and the canonical homotopy class of trivializations
of
2(L,\widetilde{\phi})
over
\partial_{1}^{c}\Sigma^{b}
similarly determine a homotopy class of trivializations
of
(V,\varphi)
over
\partial_{1}^{c}\Sigma^{b}
.
The resulting trivializations of
V^{\varphi}
and
(V,\varphi)|_{\partial_{1}^{c}\Sigma^{b}}
yield
another homotopy class of isomorphisms (
4.4
),
called the
associated spin
(or
AS
)
orientation
induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
in
[
18
, Section 3.2]
.
These two homotopy classes of isomorphisms (
4.4
) are in fact the same.

The proof of the last claim is similar to the proof of
[
18
, Corollary 3.8(1)]
,
as corrected above.
In this case, we also pinch off a pair of circles near each topological component
of
\partial_{1}^{c}\Sigma^{b}
that are interchanged by
\sigma
to form a nodal symmetric surface
(\Sigma_{0},\sigma_{0})
.
The domain of its normalization (
4.7
) now also contains
a copy
(\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1},\eta_{i})
of
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\eta)
for each topological component of
\partial_{1}^{c}\Sigma^{b}
.
The analogue of (
4.11
) now also includes isomorphisms

\det D_{(V_{i},\varphi_{i})}\approx\det\!\big(n\bar{\partial}_{(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{i},\eta_{i})}\big)

(4.13)

on the right side.
With the vertical isomorphisms corresponding to the AS orientations induced
by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
,
it again commutes up to homotopy, without any condition on
L_{0}|_{\Sigma_{0}^{+}}
in this case.
With the vertical isomorphisms corresponding to
the stabilization orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
,
the analogue of the diagram (
4.11
)
now commutes up to homotopy as well because
\deg L_{0}|_{\mathbb{P}_{i}^{1}}\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
in this case
and thus the left-hand side of (
4.12
) is even.
By Lemmas 3.3 and 3.7 in
[
18
]
, the homotopy classes of isomorphisms (
4.10
)
and (
4.13
)
corresponding to the AS and stabilization orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}

and
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
are the same.
Thus, the homotopy classes of left vertical isomorphisms in (
4.11
)
corresponding to the AS and stabilization orientations induced by
\psi_{V\oplus 2L}

and
\psi^{\prime}_{V\oplus 2L}
are the same.

4.3
Equivariant localization,
[
19
, Sections 4,5]

Let
n\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, and
\mathbf{a}\!\in\!(\mathbb{Z}^{+})^{k}
be as in
Section
4.1
and
m\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
be the integer part of
n/2
.
We denote by
\phi
the involution
\tau_{n}
or
\eta_{n}
on
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
,
with
n\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
in the latter case, and
by
X_{n;\mathbf{a}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
a
\phi
-real complete intersection of multi-degree
\mathbf{a}
,
which we assume exists.
Let

|\phi|=\begin{cases}0,&\hbox{if}~\phi\!=\!\tau_{n};\\
1,&\hbox{if}~\phi\!=\!\eta_{n}.\end{cases}

We also assume that
n\!-\!k\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and that the pair
(n,\mathbf{a})
satisfies
the conditions on real orientability of
X_{n;\mathbf{a}}
in
[
19
, Proposition 1.4]

if
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
.
In particular,

|\mathbf{a}|\equiv a_{1}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!a_{k}\cong k\mod 2.

(4.14)

The real symplectic manifold
(X_{n;\mathbf{a}},\omega_{n}|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}}},\phi|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}}})

then admits a real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
with
\deg L\!=\!(n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|)/2
;
see
[
19
, Sections 2.1/2]
and Section
4.1
above.

We denote by
\mathcal{L}\!\in\!H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
the homology class of a linearly embedded
\mathbb{P}^{1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

and by
J_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}
the standard complex structure on
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
.
The restriction of the standard
\mathbb{T}^{n}
-action on
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

to a subtorus
\mathbb{T}^{m}\!\subset\!\mathbb{T}^{n}
commutes with the involution
\phi
and
has the same fixed points

P_{1}\equiv[1,0,\ldots,0],\qquad\ldots,\qquad P_{n}\equiv[0,\ldots,0,1];

see
[
19
, Section 4.1]
.
This restriction induces a
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-action on the moduli space

\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}\big(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},d\big)\equiv\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}\big(d\mathcal{L};J_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}\big)

of real genus
g
degree
d
holomorphic maps to
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\phi)
with

\ell
conjugate pairs of marked points.
Equivariant localization contributions from some
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-fixed loci of
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},d)

to the integral
[
19
, (4.4)]
are provided by
[
19
, (4.26)]
.
This integral computes twisted real GW-invariants
of
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\omega_{n},\phi)
associated with
\mathbf{a}
.
For
k\!=\!0
, these invariants are the real GW-invariants of
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\omega_{n},\phi)

with the real orientation
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
and a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
on
L
lifting
\phi

specified in
[
19
, Section 2.2]
.
In this case,
[
19
, Theorem 4.6]
specifies the equivariant
localization contributions from all
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-fixed loci
and thus provides a way of computing these real invariants.

The proof of
[
19
, (4.26)]
is based on
the implicit presumption in
[
19
, (5.25)]
that the isomorphisms (
3.33
)
are orientation-preserving in the relevant cases.
Since the moduli spaces in
[
19
]
are oriented via (
3.22
) and
the degree
\mathfrak{d}(e)
of the real map
[f]
corresponding to
an edge
e\!\in\!\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)
is odd
if the corresponding factor in
[
19
, (4.26)]

is not zero,
the second statement of Proposition
3.12
with
g_{1},g_{2}\!=\!0
implies that
the sign of the isomorphism in
[
19
, (5.25)]

is
(-1)^{\epsilon_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{a})}
with

\epsilon_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{a})\equiv\frac{n\!-\!2\!-\!k}{2}\binom{|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)|}{2}\!+\!\frac{n\!-\!2\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}\binom{|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)|}{2}\cong\bigg(n\!+\!\frac{k\!+\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}\bigg)\binom{|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)|}{2}\mod 2\,.

However, this is of relevance for the purposes of
[
19
, (4.26)]

only if all components
a_{i}
of
\mathbf{a}
are odd.
If
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
,
|\mathbf{a}|\!\cong\!k
mod 4 then
by the conditions on real orientability of
X_{n;\mathbf{a}}
in
[
19
, Proposition 1.4]
.
If
\phi\!=\!\eta_{n}
, then the odd components
a_{i}
come in pairs and
thus
|\mathbf{a}|\!\cong\!k
mod 4 in this case as well.
It follows that the implicit presumption in
[
19
, (5.25)]
is valid in
the relevant cases.

On the other hand, the real edge contributions
\textnormal{Cntr}_{(\Gamma,\sigma);e}

with
e\!\in\!\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)
appearing in
[
19
, (4.26)]

and defined in
[
19
, (4.23)]
were obtained
in
[
19
, Section 5.4]
under the assumption that
the moduli spaces
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\ell}
are oriented as in
[
18
, Section 1.4]
.
If they are oriented as in Section
3.5
of the present paper,
the right-hand side in
[
19
, (4.23)]
should be multiplied by
(-1)
.
This does not affect the real odd-genus GW-invariants obtained
in
[
19
,
25
]
, but does change the sign of
the real even-genus GW-invariants.
By
[
19
, (1.1)]
, this makes the count of real lines through
a conjugate pair of points in
(\mathbb{P}^{2m-1},\tau_{2m})
,

\big\langle H^{2m-1}\big\rangle_{0,1}^{\mathbb{P}^{2m-1},\tau_{2m}}\equiv\big\langle\tau_{0}(\textnormal{pt})\!\big\rangle_{0,1}^{\!\omega_{2m},\tau_{2m}},

to be
-1
with respect to the canonical real orientation
specified in
[
19
, Section 2.2]
.
In light of the last claim of Corollary
3.10
,
the
2m\!=\!4
case of this statement agrees with the second equality
in the first equation in
[
5
, (13.26)]
,
as the
(\phi,\tau)
-moduli spaces in
[
5
]
are oriented
from a spin structure via the canonical isomorphism (
3.29
);
see
[
5
, (12.24)]
.
Changing the sign of the right-hand side in
[
19
, (4.23)]
also
reverses the signs of real GW-invariants
\textnormal{GW}_{g,d}^{\phi}
of
(\mathbb{P}^{3},\omega_{4},\tau_{4})

and real enumerative invariants
\textnormal{E}_{g,d}^{\phi}
of
(\mathbb{P}^{3},\tau_{4})

with
g\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
in
[
25
, Table 2]
.
For
(n,(\mathbf{a}))\!=\!(5,(5))
and
g\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, this change makes
the overall equivariant contribution
[
19
, (4.26)]

the negative of
[
27
, (3.22)]
.

Suppose now that the real moduli spaces are oriented via (
3.20
).
By the first statement of Proposition
3.12
with
g_{1},g_{2}\!=\!0
,
the right-hand sides in
[
19
, (4.26),(5.25)]

should then be multiplied by
(-1)
to the power of
\frac{n-2-k}{2}\binom{|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)|}{2}
.
The exponent
\mathfrak{s}_{v}
of the vertex sign appearing in
[
19
, (4.26)]

and defined below
[
19
, (4.17)]
accounts for
the sign of Proposition
3.13
with
B\!=\!0
if
\textnormal{val}_{v}(\Gamma)\!\geq\!3

and for the sign of Proposition
3.14

(applied
|\textnormal{E}_{v}(\Gamma)|
times if
\textnormal{val}_{v}(\Gamma)\!\geq\!3
and

|\textnormal{E}_{v}(\Gamma)|\!-\!1
times if
\textnormal{val}_{v}(\Gamma)\!=\!1,2
);
see the second half of
[
19
, Remark 4.3]
.
In this case, it thus reduces to

\mathfrak{s}_{v}=\sum_{i\in S_{v}^{-}}\!\!\big(1\!+\!b_{i}\!+\!p_{i}\big).

The conjugate edge contributions
\textnormal{Cntr}_{(\Gamma,\sigma);e}
with
e\!\in\!\textnormal{E}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)

appearing in
[
19
, (4.26)]
and defined in
[
19
, (4.21/2)]

include the sign of Proposition
3.13
;
see the sentence in
[
19
, Remark 4.4]
referencing [13, Theorem 1.4].
In this case, the expressions in
[
19
, (4.21/2)]
should thus be multiplied
by
(-1)^{(n-|\mathbf{a}|)\mathfrak{d}(e)/2-1}
.

It remains to determine the sign
(-1)^{\epsilon}
of the real edge contribution
\textnormal{Cntr}_{(\Gamma,\sigma);e}

with
e\!\in\!\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)
appearing in
[
19
, (4.26)]

and defined in
[
19
, (4.23)]
if the real moduli spaces are
oriented via (
3.20
).
This can be done under the assumption that the edge degree
\mathfrak{d}(e)

and all components
a_{i}
of
\mathbf{a}
are odd.
If
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
or
n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
, we can apply Lemma
3.1
with

g=0,\qquad\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L=1,\quad\hbox{and}\quad\deg L=-\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}\mathfrak{d}(e)

to compare the orientations induced via (
3.22
) and (
3.20
).
Taking into consideration the reversal of the orientations of the moduli spaces
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,\ell}

as above, we conclude that the sign exponent in
[
19
, (4.26)]
should be

\epsilon=|\phi|\!+\!\frac{\mathfrak{d}(e)\!+\!1}{2}\!+\!\left\lfloor{\!\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{4}\mathfrak{d}(e)\!}\right\rfloor\,.

(4.15)

By comparing with the case
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
and
n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|\!+\!2\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}

established in
[
18
, Section 5.4]
,
we show in Section
4.6
that (
4.15
) applies in the remaining case,
i.e.
\phi\!=\!\eta_{n}
and
n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|\!+\!2\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
, as well.

For any graph
(\Gamma,\sigma)
with a set
Ver
of vertices and a set
Edg
of edges
as in
[
19
, Theorem 4.6]
,

\displaystyle\big|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)\big|\!+\!2\big|\textnormal{E}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)\big|=|\textnormal{Edg}|=\sum_{v\in\textnormal{V}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\!\!\!\big|\textnormal{E}_{v}(\Gamma)\big|,

\displaystyle|\textnormal{Edg}|\!+2\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{v\in\textnormal{V}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\!\!\!\big(\mathfrak{g}(v)\!-\!1\big)=g\!-\!1,\quad\hbox{and}\quad\sum_{e\in\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\!\!\mathfrak{d}(e)\!+2\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{e\in\textnormal{E}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\!\!\mathfrak{d}(e)=d\,.

If the product in
[
19
, (4.26)]
is nonzero, then
|\mathbf{a}|\!\cong\!k
mod 4
and
\mathfrak{d}(e)\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
for every
e\!\in\!\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)
.
In such a case,
n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|,dg\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and

\begin{split}&\frac{n\!-\!2\!-\!k}{2}\binom{|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)|}{2}\!+\!\sum_{e\in\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\bigg(\!1\!+\!\left\lfloor{\!\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{4}\mathfrak{d}(e)\!}\right\rfloor\!\bigg)\!\!+\!\sum_{e\in\textnormal{E}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\bigg(\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}\mathfrak{d}(e)\!\!-\!1\!\!\bigg)\!+\!\!\sum_{v\in\textnormal{V}_{+}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\big(\mathfrak{g}(v)\!\!-\!1\!+\!|\textnormal{E}_{v}(\Gamma)|\big)\\
&\hskip 144.54pt\cong\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{4}d\bigg(\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}d\!\!-\!1\!\!\bigg)\!+\!\frac{g(g\!-\!1)}{2}\!+\!\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}dg\!+\!\big|\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)\big|\\
&\hskip 144.54pt\cong\frac{1}{2}\bigg(g\!+\!\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}d\!\bigg)\bigg(g\!+\!\frac{n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|}{2}d\!\!-\!1\bigg)\!+\!(g\!-\!1),\end{split}

with
\cong
denoting the mod 2 congruence.
By Lemma
3.1
with
\textnormal{rk}_{\mathbb{C}}L\!=\!1
and
\deg L\!=\!-(n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|)d/2
,
the first summand on the right-hand side above accounts for the difference
in orienting the first factor in (
3.29
)
via (
3.20
) vs. (
3.22
).
The second summand on the right-hand side corresponds to
the reversal of the orientation of the second factor in (
3.29
)
for
g\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.

4.4
Real GW vs. curve counts,
[
25
]

Let
(X,\omega,\phi)
be a compact real symplectic sixfold and
B\!\in\!H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
.
For
g,h\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, define
\widetilde{C}_{h,B}^{X}(g)\!\in\!\mathbb{Q}
by

\sum_{g=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{C}_{h,B}^{X}(g)t^{2g}=\bigg(\frac{\sinh(t/2)}{t/2}\bigg)^{h-1+\langle c_{1}(X,\omega),B\rangle/2}\,.

(4.16)

If
(L,[\psi],\mathfrak{s})
is a real orientation on
(X,\omega,\phi)

and

\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell}\!\in\!H^{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})
, define

\textnormal{E}_{h,B}^{X,\phi}(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell})\!\in\!\mathbb{Q}
by

\int_{[\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}(X,B)]^{\textnormal{vir}}}\!\!\big(\textnormal{ev}_{1}^{*}\mu_{1}\big)\!\ldots\!\big(\textnormal{ev}_{\ell}^{*}\mu_{\ell}\big)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq h\leq g\\
g-h\in 2\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\widetilde{C}_{h,B}^{X}\big(\tfrac{g-h}{2}\big)\textnormal{E}_{h,B}^{X,\phi}\big(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell}\big);

(4.17)

the left-hand side above is the real genus
g
degree
B
(primary) GW-invariants of
(X,\omega,\phi)

determined the insertions
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell}
.
By
[
25
, Theorem 1.1]
,
\textnormal{E}_{h,B}^{X,\phi}(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell})
is a signed count of
\phi
-invariant
J
-holomorphic genus
h
degree
B
curves
in
X
for a generic
J\!\in\!\mathcal{J}_{\omega}^{\phi}
that pass through generic representatives
for the Poincare duals of
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell}
,
if the complex line bundle
L
admits a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
lifting
\phi
and
the moduli space
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}(X,B)
is oriented via (
3.22
).
In particular,
\textnormal{E}_{h,B}^{X,\phi}(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{\ell})\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
.
As we note below, the proof in
[
25
]
contains a subtle omission.
On the other hand, the conclusion holds even if
L
does not admit
a conjugation
\widetilde{\phi}
lifting
\phi
,
but
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi}(X,B)
is oriented via (
3.20
)
and
\sinh
in (
4.16
) is replaced by
\sin
.

The last paragraph in the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 2.4]
presumes
that the isomorphism (
3.33
) in the present paper is orientation-preserving.
This is indeed the case in the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 2.4]
because

(1)

the moduli spaces of real maps in
[
25
]
are oriented
via (
3.22
);

(2)

the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 2.4]
depends on applications of
the second statement of Proposition
3.12
only with the map
\mathbf{u}_{2}
being a doublet
with the restriction of the map to each connected component of the domain
being constant and thus with
g_{2}\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
and
B_{2}\!=\!0
.

If the moduli spaces of real maps in
[
25
]
were oriented
via (
3.20
),
the isomorphism (
3.33
) in the present paper would still be orientation-preserving
in the applications in the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 2.4]

by the first statement of Proposition
3.12
with
g_{2}\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
The first statement of Proposition
3.13
would be used
with
B\!=\!0
and
S^{-}\!=\!\emptyset
.
The node-identifying immersion
[
25
, (3.21)]
,
which is a composition of the first node-identifying immersions
in (
3.36
) in the present paper,
would then be orientation-preserving by the first statement of Proposition
3.14
.
The use of the first instead of the second statements of
Propositions
3.13
and
3.14

would drop the signs of
(-1)^{\mathfrak{g}(e/w_{0})}
and
(-1)^{g_{i}}

from the right-hand sides in the last equation in the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 2.4]

and in the statement of this proposition, respectively.
The latter would also be dropped from the right-hand sides of
[
25
, (2.10)]

and of the equations in Section 2 in
[
25
]

after Proposition 2.4.
As a consequence,
\mathfrak{i}
would drop out from the last two expressions in the last equation
in
[
25
, Section 2]
.
This would then establish the statement below (
4.17
) with

\sinh
in (
4.16
) replaced by
\sin
.

4.5
Real GW-theory of local 3-folds,
[
10
]

A complex vector bundle
E
over a compact symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
gives rise to

twisted GW-invariants
of
(X,\omega)
that have played a prominent role in (complex) GW-theory.
These are the integrals of the Euler classes of the Index bundles of CR-operators
on the pullbacks of
E
over the moduli spaces
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\bullet}(X;J)

of
J
-holomorphic maps.
When
X
is a Riemann surface and
E\!=\!L_{1}\!\oplus\!L_{2}
is the direct sum of
complex line bundles
L_{1},L_{2}
,
such invariants give rise to a topological quantum field theory and encode
the local structure of standard GW-invariants of symplectic sixfolds.
Analogous integrals in the real setting, with
L_{1}\!\approx\!L_{2}
,
are set up and studied in
[
10
]
.
These integrals give rise to a Klein topological quantum field theory and should encode
the local structure of real GW-invariants of real symplectic threefolds with a certain
real orientation, in the sense of
[
17
, Definition 5.1]
or
[
10
, Definition A.1]

(i.e. Definition
1.1
in the present paper).
Contrary to what the abstract and the introduction in
[
10
]
might suggest,
the associated real orientation on
E
is not specified directly,
as it is not relevant to evaluating the integrals
[
10
, (2.10)]
.
Instead, real orientations on the symmetric Riemann surfaces
X\!=\!\Sigma

in the sense of
[
10
, Definition A.1]
are indicated in terms of
the properties of orientations they induce on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{g,\ell}^{\phi;\bullet}(X;J)
;
the associated real orientation on
E
can then be deduced from these specifications.

The explicit real orientations on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with a conjugate pair of marked points
described in the two cases of the proof of
[
10
, Proposition 5.2]
,
which are not really needed for the purposes of
[
10
]
,
are off though due to an error in the first statement of
[
10
, Lemma 5.3]
;
the correct statement is provided by Lemma
3.1
in the present paper.
With this correction, the homotopy class of isomorphism
\theta_{tw}
in the last paragraph
of Case 1 of the proof of this proposition is given by the isomorphism
\theta

in
[
10
, (5.9)]
, which is the inverse of the isomorphism (
1.6
) in the present paper,
with
L\!=\!\Sigma\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
and
\phi\!=\!\mathfrak{c}

(not the composition of
\theta
with
\textnormal{id}_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{C}}\!\oplus\!(-\textnormal{id}_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{C}})
,
as indicated by the second sentence of this paragraph).
The isomorphism
[
10
, (3.14)]
induced by the real orientation specified in this way
is the isomorphism induced by
\phi
in
[
10
, (5.3)]
.
The isomorphism
\theta
in the second and third paragraphs of Case 2 of the proof
of this proposition should be replaced
by
\{\textnormal{id}_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{C}}\!\oplus\!(-\textnormal{id}_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{C}})\!\}\!\circ\!\theta
.
On the other hand, if the orientation on
\mathbb{R}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}
in the proof of

[
10
, Proposition 5.2]
were chosen as in the present paper and in
[
9
]
,
instead of as in
[
16
,
17
]
,
the real orientations in the two cases of the proof of this proposition would have
been as indicated in its proof.
The term
solutions
in the first and third paragraphs of Case 2 in this proof refers
to real branched covers.
The only other statement in
[
10
]
which uses its Lemma 5.3 is Lemma A.3,
where
(-1)^{\iota}
should analogously be replaced by
(-1)^{\iota(\iota-1)/2}
.
The latter is in turn used only at the end of the proof of
[
10
, Proposition 5.2]
,
where
any
dependence of the comparison of Lemma A.3 on the index
\iota
alone suffices.
In particular, the statement of
[
10
, Proposition 5.2]
is not impacted in any way.

Similarly to the oversights in
[
19
]
and
[
25
]
,
the claim of the expected exponential relation between connected and disconnected
real GW-invariants below
[
10
, Theorem 1.1]
and in
[
10
, (2.16)]

implicitly presumes that the isomorphisms (
3.33
) are orientation-preserving
when
(X,\phi)\!=\!(\Sigma,c)
and thus
n\!=\!1
.
Since the moduli spaces of real maps to
(\Sigma,c)

are oriented via (
3.20
) in
[
10
]
, this is indeed the case.

4.6
Wrapup of proof of (
4.15
)

Let
n\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
,
m\!=\!n/2
,
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n},\eta_{n}
, and

c=\begin{cases}\tau,&\hbox{if}~\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}\,;\\
\eta,&\hbox{if}~\phi\!=\!\eta_{n}\,.\end{cases}

We denote by
\gamma\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
the tautological line bundle.
For
a\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
, the conjugation
\phi
on
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
lifts to a
\mathbb{C}
-antilinear automorphism

\mathfrak{c}_{\phi}\!:\gamma^{\otimes a}\longrightarrow\gamma^{\otimes a};

see
[
19
, Section 2.1]
.
If
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
or
a\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathfrak{c}_{\phi}
is a conjugation;
if
a\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathfrak{c}_{\eta}^{2}
is the multiplication by
-1
.
Define

\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;a},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;a}\big)=\big(\gamma^{*\otimes a}\!\oplus\!\phi^{*}\overline{\gamma^{*\otimes a}},\phi_{\gamma^{*\otimes a}}^{\oplus}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\phi\big).

If
\phi\!=\!\eta_{n}
, this real bundle pair is isomorphic to the real bundle pair

(2\gamma^{*\otimes a},\widetilde{\eta}_{n;1,1}^{(a)})
in
[
19
, Section 2.1]

via the map

\big(2\gamma^{*\otimes a},\widetilde{\eta}_{n;1,1}^{(a)}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;a},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;a}\big),\quad(v,w)\longrightarrow\big(v,\mathfrak{c}_{\eta}(w)\!\big).

If
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
,
(\mathcal{L}_{n;a},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;a})
is isomorphic to
the real bundle pair
2(\gamma^{*\otimes a},\mathfrak{c}_{\tau})
via the map
\Phi_{L}
in (
1.6
)
with
L\!\equiv\!\gamma^{*\otimes a}
.

Let
\mathbf{a}\!\equiv\!(a_{i})_{i\in[k]}
be a
k
-tuple of elements of
\mathbb{Z}^{+}\!-\!2\mathbb{Z}
,

n^{\prime}\!=\!n\!-\!2k
,
\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})\!=\!m\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|
, and

\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}}\big)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;a_{i}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;a_{i}}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\phi\big).

We view
\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
as being given by the first
n^{\prime}
homogeneous coordinates
so that its normal bundle

(V_{c},\varphi_{c})\equiv\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\!\big(\mathcal{L}_{n^{\prime};1},\widetilde{\phi}_{n^{\prime};1}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1},\phi|_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}\big)

corresponds to the last
2k
homogeneous coordinates.
A
\phi
-real complete intersection
X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
is
cut out by a transverse
(\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\phi)
-real section
s_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}
of
\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}
.
Thus, the sequence

0\longrightarrow\big(TX_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}},\textnormal{d}\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}\big)\longrightarrow\big(T\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\textnormal{d}\phi\big)\!\big|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}\xrightarrow{\nabla s_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}}\big)\!\big|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}\longrightarrow 0

(4.18)

of real bundle pairs over
X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}
,
where
\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}\!=\!\phi|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}
, is exact.
Along with the Euler exact sequence of
[
19
, Lemma 2.1]
,
it determines isomorphisms

\begin{split}\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(TX_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}},\textnormal{d}\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}\big)\!\otimes\!\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;|\mathbf{a}|},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;|\mathbf{a}|}\big)\!\big|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}&\approx\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(T\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\textnormal{d}\phi\big)\big|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}},\\
\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}\big(TX_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}},\textnormal{d}\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}\big)&\approx\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})}\big)\!\big|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}.\end{split}

(4.19)

The last isomorphism determines
L\!\equiv\!L_{n;\mathbf{a}}\!\equiv\!\gamma^{*\otimes\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})}

and
[\psi]
as in Definition
1.1
.
If
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
or
n\!-\!|\mathbf{a}|\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
, this isomorphism corresponds
to the isomorphisms
[
19
, (2.18)]
multiplied by
2^{n^{\prime}/2-1}

under identifications as in (
1.6
).

Let
S^{1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{1}
be an embedded circle preserved by the involution
c
and

f\!:\mathbb{P}^{1}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
be a
(\phi,c)
-real continuous map.
Let
s_{1}
be a nowhere zero section of the complex line bundle
f^{*}\gamma^{*}|_{S^{1}}
.
For each
a\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
, the bundle homomorphism

\displaystyle\Psi_{a}\!:\big(S^{1}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{2},c|_{S^{1}}\!\times\!\mathfrak{c}\big)\longrightarrow f^{*}\mathcal{L}_{n;a}\big|_{S^{1}}\!=\!\big(f^{*}\gamma^{*\otimes a}\big|_{S^{1}}\big)\!\oplus\!\big\{c|_{S^{1}}\big\}^{\!*}\overline{\big(f^{*}\gamma^{*\otimes a}\big|_{S^{1}}\big)},

\displaystyle\quad\Psi_{a}(z,c_{1},c_{2})=\big((c_{1}\!+\!\mathfrak{i}c_{2})s_{1}(z)^{\otimes a},\big(z,\overline{(c_{1}\!-\!\mathfrak{i}c_{2})}s_{1}(c(z)\!)^{\otimes a}\big)\!\big),

is then an isomorphism of real bundle pairs over
(S^{1},c|_{S^{1}})

and thus determines a trivialization of its target.
Any other nowhere zero section
s_{1}^{\prime}
of
f^{*}\gamma^{*}|_{S^{1}}
is
of the form
s_{1}^{\prime}\!=\!\rho s_{1}
for some
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-valued continuous function
\rho
on
S^{1}
.
In such a case,
\Psi_{a}^{-1}\!\circ\!\Psi_{a}^{\prime}
corresponds to the continuous function

S^{1}\longrightarrow\textnormal{GL}_{2}\mathbb{C},\quad z\longrightarrow\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&1\\
-\mathfrak{i}&\mathfrak{i}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\rho(z)^{a}&0\\
0&\overline{\rho(c(z)\!)}^{a}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&1\\
-\mathfrak{i}&\mathfrak{i}\end{array}\right)^{-1}.

For
a\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
,
\Psi_{a}^{-1}\!\circ\!\Psi_{a}^{\prime}
thus lies in the canonical homotopy class
of trivializations of
(S^{1}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{2},c|_{S^{1}}\!\times\!\mathfrak{c})
.
It follows that there is a natural correspondence, independent of the choice of
s_{1}
,
between the homotopy classes of trivializations of
f^{*}\mathcal{L}_{n;a_{1}}\big|_{S^{1}}

and
f^{*}\mathcal{L}_{n;a_{2}}\big|_{S^{1}}
whenever
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
are of the same parity.

Suppose in addition that
f
is holomorphic and

0\longrightarrow(V,\varphi)\longrightarrow f^{*}\big(T\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\textnormal{d}\phi|_{T\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}\big)\longrightarrow f^{*}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}}\big)\longrightarrow 0

(4.20)

is an exact sequence of holomorphic real bundle pairs over
(\mathbb{P}^{1},c)
.
Similarly to (
4.19
), it determines an isomorphism

\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{\textnormal{top}}(V,\varphi)\approx f^{*}\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})}\big).

(4.21)

It also determines a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\begin{split}&\big(\mathbb{P}^{1}\!\times\!\mathbb{C},c\!\times\!\mathfrak{c}\big)\!\oplus\!\big(\!(V,\varphi)\!\oplus\!f^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{n;-\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;-\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})})\!\big)\!\oplus\!f^{*}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}}\big)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\approx\frac{n^{\prime}\!+\!2}{2}f^{*}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;1},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;1}\big)\!\oplus\!(k\!-\!1)f^{*}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;1},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;1}\big)\!\oplus\!f^{*}\big(\mathcal{L}_{n;-\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;-\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})}\!\big).\end{split}

(4.22)

If
\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
, then
n^{\prime}\!+\!2\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
.
The restriction of the first summand on the right-hand side above to
S^{1}
then has
a canonical homotopy class of trivializations;
it is obtained by taking the same trivialization on each of the
(n^{\prime}\!+\!2)/2
copies
of
f^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{n;1},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;1})|_{S^{1}}
.
By the previous paragraph, the trivializations of
the
k
factors
f^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{n;a_{i}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;a_{i}})
of
f^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}})

over
S^{1}
correspond to the trivializations of the summands of
the last two terms on the right-hand side of (
4.22
).
Thus, there is a canonical homotopy class of trivializations

\big(\!(V,\varphi)\!\oplus\!f^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{n;-\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;-\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})})\!\big)\!\big|_{S^{1}}\approx\big(S^{1}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{n^{\prime}+1},c|_{S^{1}}\!\times\!\mathfrak{c}\big)\,.

(4.23)

The induced homotopy class of trivializations on the top exterior powers
corresponds to the restriction of (
4.21
) to
S^{1}
.
If
\phi\!=\!\tau_{n}
, the restrictions of these trivializations to the real parts of
the two sides determine a homotopy class of trivializations

V^{\varphi}\!\oplus\!f^{*}L_{n;\mathbf{a}}^{\,*}\big|_{S^{1}}\approx S^{1}\!\times\!\mathbb{R}^{n^{\prime}+1}.

(4.24)

The collection of such trivializations obtained by pulling back the exact sequence (
4.18
)
by continuous maps
f
with values in
X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}
determines a spin structure
\mathfrak{s}

on the real vector bundle

TX_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}^{\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}\!\oplus\!L_{n;\mathbf{a}}^{\,*}|_{X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}^{\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}}

over
X_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}^{\phi_{n;\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}}}
.
Via the map
\Phi_{L_{n;\mathbf{a}}^{*}}
as in (
1.6
), this spin structure corresponds
to the canonical spin structure provided by
[
19
, (2.26)]
.

We now also assume that
\textnormal{d}f(T\mathbb{P}^{1})\!\subset\!V
and the degree of
f
is
d
.
We denote by
\bar{\partial}_{V}
,
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}
, and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}

the standard real Cauchy-Riemann operators on
(V,\varphi)
,
f^{*}(T\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\textnormal{d}\phi|_{T\mathbb{P}^{n-1}})
,
and
f^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}},\widetilde{\phi}_{n;\mathbf{a}})
, respectively, and by

G_{c}(f)\subset\ker\bar{\partial}_{V}\subset\ker\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}=T_{f}\big(\mathfrak{P}_{0}^{\phi,c}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},d)\!\big)

the tangent space to the orbit of the
G_{c}
-action on the space of
parametrized real degree
d
holomorphic maps to
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
.
The orientation on
G_{c}
induces an orientation on
G_{c}(f)
.
The operators
\bar{\partial}_{V}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}
descend
to operators
\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}^{\prime}
on the quotients of their domains
by
G_{c}(f)
.
The operators
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}^{\prime}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}
are surjective;
the kernel of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}^{\prime}
is canonically isomorphic to the tangent space of

\mathfrak{M}_{0,0}^{\phi}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},d)
at
[f]
.
The exact sequence (
4.20
) gives rise to an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}\longrightarrow 0

of Fredholm operators over
(\mathbb{P}^{1},c)
.
This exact sequence determines an isomorphism

\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}\big)\approx\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}^{\prime}\big).

(4.25)

An orientation on the determinant of
\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}
is induced
by the canonical trivialization (
4.23
) via (
3.20
).
This orientation corresponds to the orientation of
the kernel of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}
relative to
\mathfrak{M}_{0,0}^{\phi}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},d)

for the purposes of computing the
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-equivariant contributions
to the integral
[
19
, (4.4)]
.

Let
e\!\in\!\textnormal{E}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\sigma}(\Gamma)
be a real edge as in
[
19
, (4.23)]
.
The
(\phi,c)
-real holomorphic map
f\!:\mathbb{P}^{1}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}
corresponding to the edge
e

is a degree
\mathfrak{d}(e)
cover of the line
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\vartheta(v_{1})\vartheta(v_{2})}\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

through the
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-fixed points
P_{\vartheta(v_{1})},P_{\vartheta(v_{2})}\!\in\!\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

with
\vartheta(v_{2})\!=\!\vartheta(v_{1})\!+\!1
;
it is branched over the points
P_{\vartheta(v_{1})}
and
P_{\vartheta(v_{2})}
only.
It can be assumed that
\vartheta(v_{2})\!\leq\!n^{\prime}
.
We denote by
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{V_{c}}

the standard real Cauchy-Riemann operators on
f^{*}(T\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1},\textnormal{d}\phi|_{T\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}})

and
f^{*}(V_{c},\varphi_{c})
, respectively, and by
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}^{\prime}

the operator induced by
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}
on the quotient of the domain of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}

by
G_{c}(f)
; these operators are surjective.
The exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n-1}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\bar{\partial}_{V_{c}}\longrightarrow 0

of Fredholm operators over
(\mathbb{P}^{1},c)
and the isomorphism (
4.25
) determine
an isomorphism

\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}\big)\approx\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}^{\prime}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\!\det\bar{\partial}_{V_{c}}\big).

(4.26)

We now assume that
\nu_{n}(\mathbf{a})\!\not\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathfrak{d}(e)\!\not\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
;
the former implies that
n^{\prime}\!+\!2\!\in\!4\mathbb{Z}
.
Orientations on the kernels (and thus on the determinants) of
\bar{\partial}_{V_{c}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}

can be obtained by evaluating sections and their derivatives at
0\!\in\!\mathbb{P}^{1}
;
see
[
19
, (5.39)]
.
The resulting orientations are called
complex
orientations in
[
19
, Section 5.4]
.
The
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-equivariant Euler classes of the kernels
of
\bar{\partial}_{V_{c}}
and
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}
with respect to these orientations
are given by
[
19
, (5.42)]
and
[
19
, (5.41)]
, respectively.
An orientation on the kernel (and thus on the determinant) of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}^{\prime}

is determined by a trivialization of the real bundle pair

f^{*}(T\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1},\textnormal{d}\phi|_{T\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}})
over
(S^{1},c|_{S^{1}})
.
Such a trivialization is given by
[
6
, (6.12)]
.
The
\mathbb{T}^{m}
-equivariant Euler class of the kernel of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathbb{P}^{n^{\prime}-1}}^{\prime}

with respect to the resulting orientation is given by
[
19
, (5.50)]
.
These three orientations induce an orientation on
\det\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}
via (
4.26
).
The real edge contribution
\textnormal{Cntr}_{(\Gamma,\sigma);e}
with respect to the corresponding orientation of
the kernel of
\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}_{n;\mathbf{a}}}
relative to
\mathfrak{M}_{0,0}^{\phi}(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\mathfrak{d}(e)\!)

at
[f]

would be
[
19
, (5.41)]
divided by the product of
[
19
, (5.50)]

and
[
19
, (5.42)]
.
This ratio is precisely
[
19
, (4.23)]
without the sign
and the order
\mathfrak{d}(e)
of the automorphism group of
f
in the denominator.

The sign of the real edge contribution
\textnormal{Cntr}_{(\Gamma,\sigma);e}
thus corresponds
to the comparison of the two orientations on the determinant of
\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}

obtained via (
4.25
) and (
4.26
) above.
For
(\phi,c)\!=\!(\tau_{n},\tau)
,
the two orientations have been shown to be the same if and only if
the number
\epsilon
given by (
4.15
) is even.
The Fredholm setups in the
(\phi,c)\!=\!(\tau_{n},\tau)
and

(\phi,c)\!=\!(\eta_{n},\eta)
cases can be identified by restricting
to the closed unit disk
\overline{\mathbb{D}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{C}
as in the proof of
[
13
, Proposition 3.5]
.
Under this identification, the orientations on the determinant of
\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}

obtained via (
4.25
)
and (
4.26
) in the
(\tau_{n},\tau)
case
are associated with the corresponding orientations in
the
(\eta_{n},\eta)
case.
Thus, the two orientations on the determinant of
\bar{\partial}_{V}^{\prime}
in
the
(\eta_{n},\eta)
case are also the same
if and only if
\epsilon\!\in\!2\mathbb{Z}
.
The extra
|\phi|\!=\!1
summand in the
(\eta_{n},\eta)
case is due to the reversal
of the orientation at the end of
[
17
, Section 3.2]
.
This establishes the claim made at the end of Section
4.3
.

Remark 4.2
.

In
[
19
]
, the conjugation
\tau_{n}
and
the anti-holomorphic bundle automorphism
\mathfrak{c}_{\tau}
are denoted
by
\tau_{n}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{\tau}^{\prime}
, respectively.
